### 13.7 Acceptance

The gates green: lint 0 errors and the 3 warnings; `npm test` up by the new tests, less `heaviestLeadBook`'s one; `build:prod` with no CSS or config warning, the JS a few kB larger (the prototype's (a), which kept "Heaviest lead book", added 6.49 kB); `npm audit` 0 and 0; `npm ci` 544 and 244. The four database suites at the baselines (67, 72, 356 with 29 routes, 33; 528 together). 13.6's end-to-end run. `utils/book.cjs`'s diff comments only.

### 13.8 Traps

1. **The order of the sums.** Sort as the book sorts before summing (13.2). Reusing `partnershipModel`'s sums, or summing in the store's order, fails the exact test and, on the rare book whose share falls on a half, shows another share than the book's.
2. **The share's whole** is the port's own total, not `getTotalRevenue()` or the Total Revenue card.
3. **D4 against D6.** Exposure counts each client's reporting-year revenue, so a thin client with revenue only in earlier years counts as a client at $0 (Paula's thin client in 13.3), while its strategic value uses its own latest year (D6). Show the count beside every revenue, so that client is seen. With no revenue on file both sides fall back to the calendar year, the page's in the browser's time zone and the book's in the server's (UTC on Render): only the label can then differ, for a few hours at New Year, with every figure $0.
4. **An inactive lead** reads "Name (inactive)", as the book writes it. P5 keeps one from arising through the API (a partner who leads a client cannot be deactivated); only older data or a direct database edit makes one, as the prototype's did. Their sheet opens as anyone's.
5. **A client without a lead** is one group, "no lead", last whatever its revenue, and not a button: there is no person to open.
6. **A `$0` or missing row** counts $0 and still counts the client; neither moves the reporting year.
7. **Not rated is never safe.** Only `stickinessPick`'s integers 1 to 5 are rated; anything else is not rated, never folded into "Rated 3 to 5", and the card shows the not-rated line whenever it shows the thin figure (U19).
8. **The book's text.** Nothing in WP5 changes what `renderBook` writes (constraint 10): not the exposure section, not the legend. If the definition ever changes, that is a decision of its own (T5), made with the AI's text and the page's port together.
9. **The shares can add to 99% or 101%**: the book rounds each on its own. Show the book's, never ones adjusted to 100.
10. **Lint.** The Dashboard's new `useMemo`s list their inputs, so the three expected `exhaustive-deps` warnings stay three. With "Heaviest lead book" gone, `heaviestLeadBook` and `formatRatio` are unused imports in `src/DashboardView.jsx`, and `no-unused-vars` is an error in `.eslintrc.cjs`: delete them.
11. **The card it replaces** linked to the Partnership tab; the new card opens the Dashboard's Exposure sub-tab. The Partnership tab stays one click away in the page's tabs, its Lead books table first by revenue, with the figure the old card showed.

### 13.9 Mixed versions and rollback

Page only. Netlify publishes it about a minute after the merge, and Render redeploys an API whose only change is a comment. The page reads only fields every API since the people plan's Phase 1 sends, so it works the same on any API Render could be rolled back to, and no feature name is needed (constraint 5). Rolling Netlify back alone removes the card, the sub-tab and the sheet's additions and brings "Heaviest lead book" back; nothing is stored, so nothing needs undoing.

### 13.10 Jeff, after deploy

On gbacpod.com, after a reload (the page holds the book as it loaded it; the AI tab's panel builds the book each time it opens): the exposure card where "Heaviest lead book" was, and it and its Exposure sub-tab against the `## Exposure` lines of the AI tab's "What the AI is given", figure for figure; the breakdown's "Rated for Stickiness" against the AI tab's header; a lead's name opening their sheet with their thin and not-rated clients; the Stickiness column in the Partnership tab's sheet. If no client was edited between the deploy and the check, an answer saved before the deploy does not read "on an earlier book", and takes a follow-up. `/api/health` `OK`, its features unchanged.

## 14. WP6: Where a new client fits

Section 9's sketch, expanded on 2026-09-30 against `2989622` (`main` after PR #62, WP5's code), and approved by Jeff on 2026-10-01 with U21 to U26 (section 1), each as recommended: U21 (a), U22 (a), U23 (a), U24 (a), U25 (a) and U26 (b). Placed after section 13 so that sections 10 and 11 keep their numbers. Where the sketch and the code disagree, the code wins (0.2 item 7): 14.4 and section 2 record each place.

**Size:** one to one and a half sessions under U21 (a) and U22 (a): the model and its tests half a session; the view, the store's slice and the form's prefill (U26 (b)) half to one; the end-to-end run and the docs the rest. U22 (b) adds about half a session (WP9's saving pattern: the kind's checks on both sides, `openState`, the list, a feature name, the contracts). Page only under the recommendations: no route, no schema, no feature name and no write. The files: a new `src/utils/clientFit.js` and a new `src/components/succession/ClientSandbox.jsx`; `src/utils/clientMetrics.js` (the scorer's mirror, U21), `src/components/succession/SuccessionScenario.tsx` (the third kind), `src/components/succession/ScenarioBar.jsx` (the kind's label and its line), `src/portfolioStore.js` (the slice), `src/ClientEnhancementForm.jsx` (U26 (b)); a comment in `utils/strategic.cjs`; `tests/strategic.test.mjs` and a new `tests/client-fit.test.mjs`; and the docs. **Needs:** U12 (b) (approved 2026-09-29); this section and U21 to U26 (approved 2026-10-01). **Gate:** WP9's after-deploy checks (section 12.10), which may trail WP5 by one, are due before WP6's code; WP5's (section 13.10) may trail WP6's code by one (0.2 item 1). It writes nothing and changes no schema, so the backup is no gate. WP1 runs whenever Jeff's choice arrives.

### 14.1 What the code has

Checked at `2989622`; the sandbox reuses each piece.

1. **The ranking Stage 1 uses for a new lead.** `departureModel` (`src/utils/departure.js:217-389`) ranks a client's lead candidates with `rankCandidates` (`:133-151`) over `leadPool` (`:116-118`, the active partners who are staying), on the practice areas of each partner's lead book (`areaIndex(clients).lead`, `:95-113`) and each person's total load from a ledger of every seat in the book but the departing people's (`createLedger`, `:70-89`; `totalLoad`, `:57-64`: lead effort plus the second chair's 20% share, then both seats' revenue, then the name): the client's second chair first when an active partner who is staying, then the most shared areas, then the lighter load. The partner's choice is applied unless `assignmentProblems` (`:158-182`) gives a reason, and the default then stands (`:273-277`); `candidateReason` (`:185-190`) words each place ("shares Healthcare", "lighter total load"). Nobody leaving is a legal input: the pool is then every active partner and the ledger the whole book.
2. **The second chair of a client that has none.** Stage 1 proposes a second chair only for a vacated seat: `defaultSecond` (`:301`) is the first candidate when the seat was vacated and the seat as it was otherwise, so a client whose seat was never held keeps none. The associate split (`src/utils/associateSplit.js:50-128`) is the code for an empty seat: for each client with a lead and no second chair it ranks everyone P3 allows with the same `rankCandidates`, on the areas of every client they hold and their total load (`:78-81`), lists the associates first (`:82-85`), proposes the best-ranked associate or nobody (`:86`), and applies a pick unless refused with its reason (`:88-96`); a pick of null is "not now" and adds nothing to its ledger (`:91-92`, `:98`).
3. **The loads.** `partnershipModel` (`src/utils/load.js:153-226`): each person's lead book and second-chair load as clients, reporting-year revenue and effort (the second chair's share, `:28`, `:31`), the averages over the active people in each role (`:180-189`) and each row's ratios against them (`:191-209`). Adding a client moves the lead's row, the second chair's, and the partners' average lead figures by the client's share over the active partners, so every other partner's ratio falls a little. P10 as amended (`docs/plans/people-and-second-chair.md`, P10). `resolveEffort` (`src/utils/clientMetrics.js:57-67`) prefers the API's `effort` and otherwise mirrors `getEffort` (the cadence weights at `:21-27`, an unset cadence 1, the handful 1.5), held equal for every cadence by `tests/load.test.mjs:144-149`.
4. **The scorer, and what the page mirrors of it.** `calculateStrategicValue` (`utils/strategic.cjs:149-163`): the revenue score (the client's own latest year, `getMostRecentRevenue`, `:67-90`, divided by $50,000, at most 10) × 0.5, plus the stickiness score (`getStickiness`, `:133-141`: (pick − 1) ÷ 4 × 10, or `UNRATED_STICKINESS`, 40 / 9, `:52`) × 0.5, minus the conflict penalty (`:54-56`: High 3, Medium 1, Low 0; an unknown label as Medium), kept within 0 to 10; the API rounds it to two decimals (`:181-183`). The page mirrors the effort (above) and the stickiness score (`resolveStickinessScore`, `src/utils/clientMetrics.js:75-87`, held equal by `tests/strategic.test.mjs:169-180`) and nothing else: no page code computes a strategic value, and constraint 9 allows a second copy only when a parity test holds it. The sketch's "the one scorer's page mirror" does not exist (14.4).
5. **The Scenarios tab's kinds.** `SuccessionScenario.tsx` offers two kinds (`:42-45`), asks before switching away from one that holds anything (`chooseKind`, `:83-94`, whose "entered" check at `:86` reads `stateFromStore`), and shows the bar and the kind's view (`:188-193`). The store keeps each kind's state (`src/portfolioStore.js:145-161`), clears it on logout (`:585-591`), never persists it (`:957-966`) and starts a new one with `newScenario` (`:941-951`); `openScenario` (`:866-910`) opens the two saved kinds (`:868`). The bar (`ScenarioBar.jsx`) names the kind (`:45`, `:175`) and, for a kind the API cannot save, shows Save and Save as off with one line (`:46`, `:180-182`, `:191-196`; `scenarioSavable`, `src/utils/scenarioState.js:415`), and nothing then asks about that kind's unsaved changes (`unsavedChanges`, `:418`; `src/App.jsx:22`, `:60-68`). Saving a kind is its keys and checks on both sides (`utils/scenarioState.cjs:229-237`, `:318-374`; `src/utils/scenarioState.js:340-369`, `:532-569`), the list's SQL (`utils/scenarioState.cjs:413-431`), a feature name (`server.cjs:153`, twelve today) and `routes`' contracts; `tests/scenario-state.test.mjs:90-106` holds the two sides' `KINDS` equal.
6. **The client form's picks.** A new client starts with no name, no practice area, Medium conflict risk, no cadence, not rated, no handful and one revenue row for the calendar year (`src/ClientEnhancementForm.jsx:131-144`); the vocabularies and limits are `VALIDATION_RULES` (`src/utils/validation.js:19-96`, held equal to `utils/clientRules.cjs:15-43` by `tests/client-rules.test.mjs`), an amount at most 1,000,000,000 (`:199`). The form's Succession Planning Impact preview runs the page's rules on the form's own state (`:667-721`; `enhanceClientWithSuccessionMetrics`, `src/utils/successionUtils.js:144-155`); the form's init effect fills it from the client opened or the defaults (`:122-156`).
7. **The map, and the conflict data.** The brief's value × effort map (build step 2) was never built: the Dashboard's Analysis sub-tab plots strategic value against revenue (`src/DashboardView.jsx:195-206`, `:452-497`), with no effort axis. The book holds one conflict fact per client, its conflict-risk pick (`utils/book.cjs:410`, the row's column at `:536` and `:551`, the legend's penalty at `:425`); no adverse party, matter or agency is recorded anywhere. Ask's example questions include "Who's the natural home for a $300k healthcare client?" (`src/utils/askTheBook.js:9-13`), which the AI answers from the book.
8. **The tests' patterns.** P3 on seeded random books (`tests/departure.test.mjs:65-92`, `:387-425`); the hire model's importer check (`tests/hire-scenario.test.mjs:287-293`); the random books of `tests/book.test.mjs:183-220`; the `routes` inventory at 29.

### 14.2 The design, and how it stays equal

- **A module of its own**, `src/utils/clientFit.js` (pure): `hypotheticalClient(picks, reportingYear)`, the client as the API would send one (each pick in the form's vocabulary or the form's default; one revenue row for the reporting year when an amount is typed; `effort` from `resolveEffort`; `strategicValue` rounded as the API rounds; the id `HYPOTHETICAL_CLIENT_ID`, `'new-client'`, which no stored id matches), and `clientFitModel({ people, clients, revenueOf, reportingYear, picks, choice })`, giving `{ client, revenue, effort, score, band, rank, lead, secondChair, before, after, groups, averages }`. `lead` and `secondChair` are `{ candidates, proposal, choice, problem, after }` as the departure engine shapes a seat. Only the Scenarios tab's view and the store import it (the hire model's rule).
- **The lead is Stage 1's answer.** The same calls with nobody leaving: `leadPool(people)` over `rankCandidates` on `areaIndex(clients).lead` and a ledger of every seat in the book, no second chair to prefer; the choice through `assignmentProblems`, the proposal standing when it is refused. A test holds it to the engine by construction (14.6): a new client is a client whose lead is leaving before they ever had one, so `departureModel` with the client added under a ghost partner who is marked as leaving gives, as its one decision, the same candidates in the same order with the same reasons and loads, the same fate for the choice and the same seat; the prototype found them equal on every fixture and random book (14.4). Fit ranks before load, as Stage 1 ranks (trap 1).
- **The second chair is the associate split's answer (U23 (a)).** `secondChairPool(people, nobody, lead.id)` ranked on `areaIndex(clients).any` and the ledger, the associates first, then everyone else P3 allows; the proposal the best-ranked active associate, or none; "None" and any of them pickable, a refused pick showing its reason with the proposal standing. Held to `associateSplitModel` over the book with the client added under its lead and every other open seat "not now", whose proposal for the client is then made over the book as it is (14.6). Under U23 (b) it would be Stage 1's ranking over everyone instead; under (c) none.
- **The loads.** `before` is `partnershipModel(people, clients, revenueOf)`, the Partnership tab's figures and the book's rows (`tests/book.test.mjs`); `after` the same with the client and its two seats. The table's rows are everyone active or holding a seat, the partners heaviest first (as the hire view lists them) and the others by name, each with both seats' count, revenue and effort before and after and the ratio against the role's average before and after; a line under it gives the partners' average lead effort, revenue and count before and after. The new client's full effort lands on the lead and the 20% share on the second chair (P10 as amended); nothing else moves, and the average moves by the client's effort over the active partners (14.6).
- **The strategic value on the page (U21 (a)).** `resolveStrategicValue` in `src/utils/clientMetrics.js`, beside `resolveEffort` and `resolveStickinessScore`: the API's `strategicValue` when the client carries one, else the formula from the raw pick (never the API's rounded `stickinessScore`) and the client's own latest revenue year (`mostRecentRevenue`, a mirror of `getMostRecentRevenue` in both revenue shapes). `tests/strategic.test.mjs` holds it to `calculateStrategicValue` (14.6), and a comment in `utils/strategic.cjs` beside the weights names the mirror and the test, so a weight is changed in both places or the test fails. The sandbox shows the score with its parts, as the brief asks ("Revenue 5.0 + Stickiness 2.8 − Conflict 1"). Under (b) a `POST` route would score the client without writing (a contract, a feature name, and a sandbox that works only once the API has deployed); under (c) the sandbox shows effort and no score.
- **The year (D4 against D6).** The typed amount is the client's revenue in the reporting year, the year the Partnership tab and the loads use (D4), and its label says so ("Revenue 2026"); for the score it is the client's own latest year (D6), which for a client with one row is the same year. When the partner adds the client (U26), the form's revenue row carries that year. In January before the new sheet, the reporting year is last year's, as every tab shows it.
- **An unrated Stickiness** scores the stand-in, 40 / 9, and the sandbox says beside the score what the book's legend says: the same for every unrated client, not a rating; the exposure line then reads "not rated (unknown, never safe)" (T5). The stickiness picker offers Not rated, as the form does.
- **"Where it lands" (U24 (a))**, in words: the effort and the score with its parts, where the client would rank among the book's clients by strategic value, by effort and by reporting-year revenue ("2nd of 14 by strategic value"), and its exposure band (thin for a pick of 1 or 2, not rated, or rated 3 to 5). No chart.
- **"Any conflict flag" (U25 (a))** is the partner's own pick, shown with its penalty in the score and flagged when High; the book holds nothing a conflict check could read.
- **Where it sits, and what the store keeps (U22 (a)).** A third kind under "What to model", "A new client", beside "Someone leaves" and "Add an associate". The store gains `clientSandbox: { picks, choice }` (the picks as typed; the choice `{ leadId?, secondChairId? }` as the engine takes one), `scenarioKind` `'client'`, `setSandboxPicks`, `setSandboxChoice` and `resetSandbox` ("Start over"); `newScenario('client')` starts one; logout clears it; `partialize` leaves it out (P11 keeps it across tabs, as the other kinds). `chooseKind` asks before clearing a sandbox that holds anything, reading the slice (its "entered" check knows only the two saved kinds); `openScenario` clears it when a saved scenario opens. The bar stays, with the kind's label, Save and Save as off, and one line in place of the saved-by line: "A new client is not saved as a scenario. When you want the client, add it on Client Details." (`scenarioSavable` false for the kind, so nothing asks about it on logout or leaving, as for a hire on an older API); New and Open work as they do. `KINDS`, `stateFromStore`, `checkState` and the list are untouched: the kind never reaches `/api/scenarios`. Under U22 (b) the kind is saved with S12 instead: `kind: 'client'` with the picks and the choice on both sides, `openState` noticing a client of that name now in the book, the list naming the client, a feature name `client-scenarios` beside `hire-scenarios` (an API without it refuses the kind), and `routes`' contracts.
- **Nothing is written** (P9; the brief's "no swap optimizer"). No route, no state on the server, no feature name; the hypothetical client is never in the book, the AI's prompts, the People list or another tab, and its id never reaches the API. A partner who wants the client adds it on Client Details: under U26 (b), "Add on Client Details" puts the picks, the reporting year's revenue row, the lead and the second chair in the store as `clientDraft`, opens Client Details with the form open on a new client, and the form's init effect reads the draft once, in place of its defaults, then clears it; the save is the form's own `POST`, its checks and its `form` history row included. Nothing is written until the partner presses Save there.
- **The AI is untouched** (constraint 10): `utils/book.cjs` does not change, `GET /api/ai/book` writes the same bytes (14.6 checks it), no thread stops taking follow-ups and no answer reads "on an earlier book" from this deploy. Ask already answers the sandbox's question from the book; the sandbox is the page's own arithmetic, with no call.
- **T3 holds:** the server never loads the page's code. The mirror runs the page's way, copying the scorer, as the exposure copies the book; both pairs are held by tests (constraint 9, which the code PR extends with the strategic value).
- **No API change, no feature name** (constraint 5): every input is in `GET /api/data/clients` (the nested `lead` and `secondChair`, `practiceArea`, `effort`, `strategicValue`, `stickiness`, `conflict_risk`, `revenues`) and `GET /api/people`, as every API since the people plan's Phase 1 sends them; the page computes the reporting year itself (D4).

### 14.3 What the sandbox shows

Under U21 (a), U22 (a), U23 (a), U24 (a), U25 (a) and U26 (b). The mocks are the prototype's figures (14.4) on its synthetic book of 13 tree-named clients on production's older tables, printed by the prototype's own renderer; the code session lays them out as the hire view lays out its cards. The tab's chooser gains the third button; with it chosen the bar shows "New scenario", the kind's badge, Save and Save as off and the line of 14.2, and the view is:

- **The picks card**, "A new client": Name (a label; "New client" until typed), Revenue <year> (an amount, blank for none), Practice areas (the form's twelve as checkboxes, as the hire's focus), Cadence (the five and Not set), Handful, Stickiness (1 to 5 and Not rated, with the book's labels) and Conflict risk (Low, Medium, High); the store re-derives everything as they change. Under them, the client's own line:

  ```
  Hemlock Health: $300,000 in 2026, Healthcare, Monthly, Stickiness not rated, conflict risk Medium
  Effort 2 (7th of 14 clients by effort). Strategic value 4.2 = revenue 6.0 × 0.5 + stickiness 4.44 × 0.5 − conflict 1
  (2nd of 14 by strategic value; 1st of 14 by revenue in 2026). Not rated: 4.44 is the stand-in every unrated client
  scores with, not a rating. Exposure: not rated (unknown, never safe).
  ```

- **The lead**: the active partners in the engine's order, each with the reason and the total load it was ranked on, the proposal marked, and a picker (a `NativeSelect` of the same list) for the partner's choice; a refused pick shows its reason under the picker and the proposal stands, as Stage 2 shows one.

  Partners

  On the same book, a $50,000 Energy client at Daily with a handful (effort 7.5, Stickiness 2, High) proposes Paula (shares Energy, the lightest of the three Energy leads), whose row reads `3 → 4 leads, 3.5 → 11 effort, 0.7× → 1.7×`, with Ash proposed as second chair (`0.7 → 2.2`) and the average `5.3 → 6.5`; a client with no area, no cadence and no revenue, rated 4, proposes Joe (the lightest partner), at effort 1 and strategic value 3.8 (stickiness 7.5 × 0.5), exposure "rated 3 to 5".

### 14.4 What the prototype found

On 2026-09-30, scratch scripts in the session's scratchpad, never committed: the model as 14.2 describes it over the repository's own `src/utils` modules, two oracles, and runs on the fixture books, on seeded random books and on a real API.

- **The lead is Stage 1's, by construction.** On `tests/fixtures/books.mjs`, on the hire suite's book (which has practice areas) and on 300 seeded random books (2 to 12 people with random roles and active flags, 0 to 40 clients with areas, picks, cadences, handfuls, conflict risks and several years, half scored as the API scores them; random picks, a blank or odd amount among them; random choices, a third of them ones P3 refuses), the model's lead candidates equalled the ghost departure's in order, reason and load, and so did the choice's fate (the reason, or the seat) on every book: 6 books had no active partner (nothing proposed, and the model says so), 103 a choice refused with its reason. The second chair's candidates, proposal and pick equalled the associate split's with the other open seats "not now" on every book; 113 books had no active associate, so no proposal (a partner or the emeritus could still be picked), None was picked in 46 runs, and 114 runs ended with no second chair. Every load after equalled the engine's given the same seats, P3 held on every book, no row but the two seats' holders moved, and the partners' average lead effort moved by the client's effort over the active partners whenever a partner led it. The model runs in 0.3 to 0.7 ms on the fixture books and in 0.66 ms per run on a 500-client book.
- **Fit before load.** On 53 of the 300 books the proposed lead was not the lightest partner, and on 25 it was the heaviest: Stage 1's rule ranks a shared practice area above any load. On the hire suite's book a $300,000 Healthcare client ranks Kevin, at a total load of 18.4 (his lead book 2.7× the partners' average), above Mike at 1.2 (0.1×), because Kevin leads Healthcare clients and Mike does not; on the synthetic book of the end-to-end run, Brendan (5.0, 1.0×) is proposed over Joe (0.5) for the same client. The view shows the reason and every load, and the partner decides (trap 1).
- **A second chair for a never-held seat.** Stage 1's engine, asked about the new client through the ghost, leaves its second chair empty (`defaultSecond`, `src/utils/departure.js:301`) and would rank everyone by fit then load; the associate split proposes the best-ranked active associate. Under Stage 1's rule (U23 (b)) the $50,000 Energy client's second chair would be Kevin, the second-heaviest partner, because he holds Energy clients; under the split's (U23 (a)) it is Ash, the associate.
- **The scorer's mirror.** `resolveStrategicValue` equalled `calculateStrategicValue` on 5,220 clients: the fixture of `tests/strategic.test.mjs`, the fixture books and a 546-client grid of picks (none, odd values and text among them), conflict labels (an unknown one and none among them) and amounts, each in three revenue shapes (rows; the `revenue` object; both, and empty rows with an object), and 3,000 seeded random clients with text amounts, out-of-range years, ties on year and the camelCase spelling of the conflict risk; the API's rounded `strategicValue` preferred when present, and the mirror's value rounded as the API rounds. A mirror with the stickiness weight at 0.6 disagrees with the scorer on 430 of the 555 fixture clients, one with $40,000 a point on 146, one with the High penalty at 2 on 72: the test is meaningful.
- **End to end on production's older tables** (integer ids): a throwaway database, `server.cjs`, an account, one associate added through `POST /api/people`, and 13 tree-named clients imported through `POST /api/data/process-csv` as the page posts it (the seeded partners, the emeritus and the associate in the seats, every pick and none, every cadence, a handful, a firm credit, two revenue years). The model over `GET /api/data/clients` and `GET /api/people` gave 14.3's figures for three clients (13 to 17 ms for the first call, the JIT's, then under 1 ms), the lead as the engine ranks it and the second chair as the split proposes it; the `clients`, `client_revenues` and `people` tables and `client_changes` (13 rows, the import's) were byte for byte as before, `GET /api/ai/book` the same text (SHA-256 `342b758964bf…`, 7,465 characters), and no hypothetical client's name in it.
- **Plan against code (0.2 item 7).** (1) The sketch's "the one scorer's page mirror" does not exist: `src/utils/clientMetrics.js` mirrors the effort and the stickiness score only (U21). (2) The sketch ranks "the partners ... as a lead would be in a departure" and says nothing of the second chair; Stage 1's engine gives a never-held seat no second chair, so a proposal is the associate split's case (U23). (3) The brief's "where it lands on the map" names a map that was never built, and "any conflict flag" data the book does not hold (U24, U25). (4) The sketch's "probably a third kind of scenario ... so it can be saved" is U22, where the recommendation is the kind without the saving. (5) The sketch's size, one and a half to two sessions, assumed the saving; without it, one to one and a half.
- **Found: candidate (ae).** The Dashboard's "Strategic Value vs Revenue" chart says under itself "Bubble size represents revenue. Top-right quadrant shows high-value, efficient clients." (`src/DashboardView.jsx:493`); the chart has no size axis (no `ZAxis`; every point the same size), and its axes are revenue and strategic value, so the top right is high revenue and a high score, not efficiency, which is the Partnership tab's revenue per effort. Cosmetic, one line; not in WP6.

### 14.5 Expected outcomes

- Scenarios' third kind, "A new client" (U22 (a)): `ClientSandbox.jsx` as 14.3 shows it, from `clientFitModel` over the store's clients and People list at `getReportingYear()`, memoised on them and the slice; the store's `clientSandbox`, `scenarioKind` `'client'`, its three actions, `newScenario('client')`, logout and `openScenario` clearing it, `partialize` unchanged; `chooseKind` reading the slice; the bar's label and line, Save and Save as off for the kind, New and Open as they are.
- The second chair proposed by the split's rule (U23 (a)), with None and everyone P3 allows in the picker.

- "Add on Client Details" (U26 (b)): `clientDraft` in the store, read once by the form's init effect when no client is open, the form otherwise as it is; the save the form's own.

**Not in this package:** saving the sandbox (U22 (b), not chosen); a succession-metrics preview for the prospect (the form shows one once the client is added); a change to Stage 1's ranking (trap 1); any change to T5, the book's text, the scorer's weights or P10; candidates (aa) to (ae); WP1.

### 14.6 Tests

- `tests/strategic.test.mjs`: `resolveStrategicValue` equal to `calculateStrategicValue` on `fixtureClients()` in the three revenue shapes and on 3,000 seeded random clients (14.4's cases), `mostRecentRevenue` equal to `getMostRecentRevenue`, the API's rounded figure preferred, and the mirror rounded as the API rounds. Shown failing first on a copy of the mirror with a weight moved, as 13.6 showed the exposure without the sort (on `origin/main` the new test only fails to find the function, which proves nothing).
- A new `tests/client-fit.test.mjs`: `hypotheticalClient` from picks (each default, a bad cadence or pick as none, a blank or bad amount as no row, its effort equal to `getEffort` and its value to the scorer's); the lead equal to Stage 1's engine through the ghost on the fixture books and 300 seeded random books with random picks and choices (the candidates in order with their reasons and loads, the choice's fate, the seat, `noCandidate`); the second chair equal to `associateSplitModel` with the other open seats "not now" (the candidates in its order, the proposal, a pick applied or refused with its reason, None); P3 on every book; the loads after equal to the engine's given the same seats, no row but the two seats' moving, the average moving by effort over the active partners, and a book with no active partner proposing nothing; the rank lines and the band; purity; and the importer check, only `ClientSandbox.jsx` and the store importing the model, and nothing in `utils/` requiring `src/` (T3). Each assertion shown failing first on a copy of the model with the rule changed (fit after load; the second chair from Stage 1's ranking).
- `tests/scenario-state.test.mjs`, `tests/hire-scenario.test.mjs`, `tests/departure.test.mjs` and `tests/load.test.mjs` unchanged and passing; no `routes`, `import-db`, `schema` or `eval-ai` change, each suite at its count (the inventory at 29).
- End to end, the scripts in the session's scratchpad as for every package: a local `server.cjs` on production's older tables with 14.4's synthetic book, and the Vite dev server, in headless Chromium: (1) the sandbox's ranking, second chair, load table and averages, read from the page, equal to the model's over `GET /api/data/clients` for the three clients of 14.3; (2) a changed pick re-ranking at once, a refused lead pick showing its reason with the proposal standing, None as second chair; (3) a tab switch keeping the sandbox, "Start over" clearing it, logout clearing it; (4) the bar with Save and Save as off and its line, New asking and Open opening a saved departure scenario after asking; (5) "Add on Client Details" opening the form prefilled and the save creating the client through `POST` with one `form` history row, the sandbox's own picks; (6) the `clients` table and `GET /api/ai/book` byte for byte unchanged until that save; (7) `origin/main`'s page showing two kinds and the branch's three, the server the same.

### 14.7 Acceptance

The gates green: lint 0 errors and the 3 warnings; `npm test` up by the new tests; `build:prod` with no CSS or config warning, the JS a few kB larger; `npm audit` 0 and 0; `npm ci` 544 and 244. The four database suites at the baselines (67, 72, 356 with 29 routes, 33; 528 together). 14.6's end-to-end run. `utils/strategic.cjs`'s diff comments only; `utils/book.cjs`, `server.cjs`, `init-db.sql` and `routes/` untouched.

### 14.8 Traps

1. **Fit before load.** Stage 1's rule ranks a partner who leads one client in the area above an idle partner without it, whatever their loads (14.4: Kevin at 2.7× above Mike at 0.1×). The sandbox's whole question is fit and capacity, so show the reason and the load on every row, and never hide the rest of the list behind the proposal. Changing the rule changes Stage 1 (one ranking, `rankCandidates`); if the partners find it wrong here, that is a decision of its own, recorded for Jeff, not a WP6 change.
2. **Ties rank by name.** With no shared area, equal loads rank by revenue, then by name: on `tests/fixtures/books.mjs`, whose clients have no practice areas, three idle partners tie at 0 and Jeff is proposed by the alphabet. Show equal loads as equal; a partner should see a toss-up as one.
3. **The average moves.** The lead's ratio rises and every other partner's falls a notch, with no change to their books; show the averages' line, or a partner reads a fall as relief.
4. **The year.** The amount counts in the reporting year (D4) and is the score's year (D6) at once; label it with the year, as the Partnership tab labels revenue. In January before the new sheet the reporting year is last year's.
5. **The stand-in.** Never show 4.44 as a rating; say beside it what the legend says. "Not rated" is a band of its own on the exposure line, never "rated 3 to 5".
6. **A second chair only from the active associates** (U23 (a)): with none at the firm the seat is proposed empty and the picker offers everyone; the share on an associate can read as a large ratio against the associates' average (Ash 0.7 → 2.2 on a Daily client with a handful).
7. **No active partner:** nothing to propose, and the view says so (`noCandidate`); the load table still shows the book.
8. **The hypothetical never reaches the API:** no write, no scenario state, no prompt; `HYPOTHETICAL_CLIENT_ID` is text no stored id matches on either table shape, and the importer check keeps the model out of every other module.
9. **The weights in two places** (U21 (a)): `utils/strategic.cjs` and the mirror, bound by the test; the comment beside the weights names both. A change to one side alone fails `npm test`.
10. **The kind is not a saved kind:** `KINDS`, `stateFromStore`, `checkState` and `tests/scenario-state.test.mjs:90-106` stay as they are; `scenarioSavable` is false for it, so logout and leaving ask nothing about it; `chooseKind` reads the slice, not `stateFromStore`, for "entered"; `openScenario` clears the slice.
11. **The draft** (U26 (b)): the form's init effect runs on the client it opens; a draft is read once, when no client is open, and cleared, or a second Add would refill a form the partner has since edited; an open stored client wins over a draft.
12. **Ranks compare rounded figures:** the book's clients carry the API's two-decimal `strategicValue`, so the hypothetical's is rounded the same way before it is placed (14.2).
13. **Lint.** The new `useMemo`s list their inputs, so the three expected `exhaustive-deps` warnings stay three; `no-unused-vars` is an error.

### 14.9 Mixed versions and rollback

Page only under U22 (a). Netlify publishes it about a minute after the merge, and Render redeploys an API whose only change is a comment. The page reads only fields every API since the people plan's Phase 1 sends and computes everything itself, so it works the same on any API Render could be rolled back to, and no feature name is needed (constraint 5). Rolling Netlify back alone removes the kind; nothing is stored, so nothing needs undoing. Under U22 (b) the kind would need `client-scenarios` in `/api/health`, and an older API would refuse its save with 400, which the bar already handles for a hire.

### 14.10 Jeff, after deploy

On gbacpod.com, after a reload: Scenarios offers "A new client"; type a prospect (a made-up one is fine) with a practice area and a cadence, and read the proposed lead with its reason, the other partners in order, the second chair, and the load table; the "before" figures against the Partnership tab, row by row; the averages' line moving; switch tabs and back (the sandbox kept); a refused pick (an associate as lead) showing its reason; "Add on Client Details" opening the form prefilled, then Cancel (nothing written: the client list unchanged); the bar with Save and Save as off and its line. `/api/health` `OK`, its features unchanged (twelve). If no client was edited between the deploy and the check, an answer saved before the deploy does not read "on an earlier book", and takes a follow-up.

## 15. WP10: The open candidates (aa) to (ae)

Written 2026-10-01 against `4041df1` (`main` after PR #64, WP6's code): the five candidates the Tier 3 packages recorded in section 2's notes and did not fix in passing (0.2 item 7), (aa) from WP2, (ab) from WP4, (ac) from WP9, (ad) from WP5 and (ae) from WP6, listed in section 4.8. Approved by Jeff on 2026-10-01, U27 to U31 (section 1) each as recommended, before the section's PR merged, so the code is in the same PR, #65 (one package, one PR: 0.2 item 1), merged as `431fb29` on 2026-10-01. Placed after section 14 so that sections 10 and 11 keep their numbers. Each candidate is given as the code has it, whether it is worth a change at all, and the smallest fix; (aa) was reproduced, not reasoned about (15.4).

**Size:** half a session to one under the recommendations: (aa)'s test is most of it (its fix is one line); (ac) is a deletion proven by the bundle; (ad) fourteen lines over seven files; (ae) two lines; (ab) a runbook paragraph; then the end-to-end run and the docs. One route changes ((aa), `POST /api/data/process-csv`), so `tests/routes.test.mjs` changes in the same PR, on both table shapes (constraint 8); the rest is page only, with no route, schema, feature name or write. The files: `data.cjs` (one line and its comment), `src/App.css`, `src/App.jsx`, `src/DashboardView.jsx`, `src/ClientListView.jsx`, `src/ScenarioModeler.jsx`, `src/components/succession/ImpactAnalysisWorkbench.jsx`, `src/DataUploadManager.jsx`, `tests/routes.test.mjs`, `deploy/README.md` and the docs. **Needs:** this section and U27 to U31. **Gate:** WP6's after-deploy checks (section 14.10) may trail it by one (0.2 item 1); nothing writes and no schema changes, so the backup is no gate. WP1 runs whenever Jeff's choice arrives.

### 15.1 What the code has

Checked at `4041df1`.

1. **(aa) The import's lock order.** `POST /api/data/process-csv` (`data.cjs:152`) opens its transaction (`:185`), takes the client-name advisory lock (`:194`), reads the People list `FOR SHARE` only when the file has a `Lead` column (`:199-201`), and then locks every stored client the file names: `SELECT * FROM clients WHERE LOWER(...) = ANY($1) FOR UPDATE` (`:223-228`), with no `ORDER BY`, so the rows are locked in the order the scan returns them: a sequential scan (there is no index on the expression; 15.4's `EXPLAIN`), the heap's order, in which an update moves a row behind every row inserted since (PostgreSQL writes a new version of the row). Pass 2a then updates those rows in one statement (`:390-398`). A rename (`PUT /api/people/:id`, `routes/people.cjs:63`) locks the person `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` (`:88-91`) and then the clients it rewrites in id order (`:121-124`, Tier 3 WP2, (s)) before its three updates (`:125-133`). Every other client write locks one client (`lockClient`, `data.cjs:594-611`; the second-chair route, `:989-995`), after reading the people it assigns `FOR SHARE` (`:568-575`), so the import and the rename are the only two writes that lock several, and in different orders. With a `Lead` column the import's `FOR SHARE` read of the People list conflicts with the rename's `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` on the person, so one waits for the other before any client is locked; without one, nothing orders them. When they deadlock, PostgreSQL aborts the import's transaction (`40P01`), the route logs `Error processing a CSV import: {"code":"40P01","message":"deadlock detected"}` through `logError` and answers 500 `Failed to process CSV data`, nothing written, and the rename completes (15.4). Check file takes the same locks (`dryRun`).
2. **(ab) PostgreSQL's own log.** `utils/errorLog.cjs` (WP2, (c)) logs a failed query's `code`, `message`, `constraint`, `table` and `column`, never `detail`, so the API's log on Render prints no row. The database's own log is another file. Under PostgreSQL's default `log_error_verbosity` a `CHECK` (23514) or `NOT NULL` (23502) violation logs `ERROR`, `DETAIL: Failing row contains (...)`, every column of the refused row, and `STATEMENT`, the SQL as sent, which for the API's parameterized writes shows `$1`, `$2` and no value (`log_parameter_max_length_on_error` is 0 by default); `terse` logs no `DETAIL`, `verbose` adds the SQLSTATE and the source location; the client is sent `detail` whatever the setting (15.4). The rows that carry text a partner typed: `clients` (`name NOT NULL`, `init-db.sql:19`; the second-chair `CHECK`, `:161`), with `notes`; `ai_answers` (`kind`'s `CHECK`, `answer` and `model` `NOT NULL`, `:172-179`), with the question and the answer; `client_changes` (`:211-216`), with each field's from and to, a note's included; `scenarios` (`:234-236`), with the state; `people` (`:124-125`), a name; `users` (`:7-8`), a password hash. A unique violation prints the key's value (`users.username`; `people (lower(name))`, `:130`) and a foreign-key violation an id. The API refuses each of those fields before it writes (`checkClient`, `validateAssignment`, `validatePersonInput`, `checkScenario`, `KINDS`, `SOURCES`), so on production such a line follows a defect or a direct SQL. The parameter's context is `superuser` (`pg_settings`): a role that is not one gets `permission denied to set parameter` from `SET`, from `ALTER DATABASE ... SET` and from `ALTER ROLE ... SET`, and can set it only after a superuser's `GRANT SET ON PARAMETER log_error_verbosity TO <role>` (PostgreSQL 15 and later; checked on this container's 16.14, 15.4); `log_min_error_statement` is the same. Whether Render's role is a superuser or holds the grant, and whether Render's dashboard shows the database's log at all, a session cannot see (WP4's note). This container's log after today's suites holds 40 such `DETAIL` lines, every one a synthetic row the `schema` suite refused on purpose.
3. **(ac) The dead `.dark` rules.** `src/App.css` has one `@layer base` (`:5-127`) holding `:root` (`:6-27`), three `.dark` rules, the HSL set (`:29-49`) and two identical `oklch` sets (`:52-84`, `:86-118`), and the `*` and `body` rules (`:120-126`); the `oklch` sets also declare `--chart-1` to `--chart-5` and `--sidebar-*`, which `tailwind.config.js` never reads (its colours are the `hsl(var(--...))` entries, `:9-43`). The file is imported by `src/main.jsx:4` and `src/App.jsx:17`. Tailwind 3 (`^3.3.3`) emits a rule written in a layer only when its selector appears in the content files (`index.html`, `src/**`); no file there carries a `dark` class, `darkMode` is unset, so the `dark:` utilities (`src/DashboardView.jsx:257`'s `dark:bg-gray-900`, for one) compile to `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` rules, which the build holds, and the built CSS (`dist/assets/index-CAFf2zvd.css`) has no `.dark{` rule, no `oklch` and no `--chart-` or `--sidebar` variable. WP9 found them with `npx tailwindcss`; 15.4 confirms it by the bundle.
4. **(ad) Revenue with `toLocaleString()`.** `formatMoney` (`src/utils/load.js:70-73`) writes whole dollars after settling to 12 significant digits, in `en-US`, as the book's does (`utils/book.cjs:359-370`), so the Partnership tab, the exposure card and sub-tab, the lead sheet, the load tables, the sandbox and the exports all read "$540,752" for the same sum. Twelve places write a revenue figure with `toLocaleString()` alone, which prints the figure's cents (up to three decimals) in the browser's locale: the header's total (`src/App.jsx:105`; `getTotalRevenue()`, `src/portfolioStore.js:657-662`, the reporting-year sum in the API's order); the Dashboard's Total Revenue card (`src/DashboardView.jsx:300`, from `:172`), its scatter tooltip (`:281`; a client's `getClientRevenue`, `:196`), its practice-area pie tooltip (`:403`) and its top-clients table (`:656`); each client card on Client Details (`src/ClientListView.jsx:344`); the Scenarios tab's header (`src/ScenarioModeler.jsx:31`, used at `:85`; `getTotalRevenue()` at `:19`); and Stage 1's chart tooltip, its "Revenue at Risk" pie tooltip, its practice-area breakdown, its affected-client list and its selected client's "Annual Revenue" (`src/components/succession/ImpactAnalysisWorkbench.jsx:307`, `:443`, `:456`, `:540`, `:749`). 13.4 found the first six ("$540,751.5" beside the exposure card's "$179,501"); the other six are found here. Data Upload's two figures are of another kind: `yearsWithTotals` (`src/DataUploadManager.jsx:55-60`) writes each year's total of the file's amounts (`summary.revenueTotals`), which runbook 7.4 step 2 compares with the sheet's `=SUM` under each year column, to the cent; and the success card's "total revenue of $..." (`:318`) is `summary.totalRevenue` (`data.cjs:493`), the sum over the file's clients of each one's `averageRevenue`, its latest year among the file's years rounded to the dollar (`utils/strategic.cjs:185`), a figure no other view shows. Every figure above agrees with the book's to the cent; only the writing differs.
5. **(ae) The scatter caption.** The Dashboard's Analysis sub-tab's "Strategic Value vs Revenue" (`src/DashboardView.jsx:452-497`) is a `ScatterChart` of `scatterData` (`:195-206`): `x` the client's reporting-year revenue (`getClientRevenue`), `y` its `strategicValue` (the API's), each point filled by its first practice area (`:482-487`; `COLORS`, `:125`); no `ZAxis` is imported (`:17-28`), so every point is the same size. The caption (`:491-495`) reads "Bubble size represents revenue. Top-right quadrant shows high-value, efficient clients.": there is no bubble size, and the top right is high revenue and a high score, not efficiency, which is revenue per effort (the Partnership tab) and on no axis. Found with it: the x axis's `unit="$"` (`:463-469`) is appended to each tick by Recharts 2.15.4 (`node_modules/recharts/es6/cartesian/CartesianAxis.js:273`), with no thousands separator, so its ticks read `0$`, `80000$`, `160000$` (15.4); the tooltip (`:274-287`) writes the revenue with `toLocaleString()` ((ad)).

### 15.2 The design, and the smallest fix for each

- **(aa), U27 (a): `ORDER BY id` on the import's `FOR UPDATE` read** (`data.cjs:227`), one line: the rows are then locked in id order, the rename's order (`routes/people.cjs:122`), on integer and uuid ids alike, the id never cast (constraint 1); PostgreSQL puts `LockRows` above the `Sort` (15.4's `EXPLAIN`), which is what the rename already relies on. Two imports still serialize on the advisory lock (`:194`); an import with a `Lead` column still waits at the People list (`:199-201`); every other client write locks one row. Nothing in the response, the contract's keys or the page changes; the route's contract gains the reproduction (15.6). Under (c) the People list would be read `FOR SHARE` on every import, which only moves the wait; under (b) nothing changes, and section 11 records it.
- **(ab), U28 (a): record and leave.** No code. Runbook 9.1 ("Where") gains a paragraph: the API's log prints no row since WP2; the database's own log, if Render shows one, prints a refused row under PostgreSQL's default verbosity; the two read-only queries that tell Jeff whether the role could change it; and what `terse` would also hide. Section 11 lists (ab) with the rest left for later. Under (b) the runbook also gives the one `ALTER DATABASE` and what each answer means.
- **(ac), U29 (a): delete `src/App.css:29-118`**, the three rules whole, leaving `:root`, `*` and `body` in the layer. Proof: the production build's CSS and JS are byte for byte the files at `4041df1` (`index-CAFf2zvd.css`, `index-CNs0yfvx.js`; Vite names each by its content), as 15.4 found; no test is needed where the bundle's names are the test.
- **(ad), U30 (a): `formatMoney` at the twelve revenue sites**, imported where the file lacks it (`src/App.jsx`, `src/ClientListView.jsx`, `src/ScenarioModeler.jsx`, whose `formatCurrency` goes; `src/DashboardView.jsx` and `ImpactAnalysisWorkbench.jsx` already import it), and Data Upload's two totals written to the cent in `en-US` (`toLocaleString('en-US', { minimumFractionDigits: 2, maximumFractionDigits: 2 })`), since the year totals are compared with a spreadsheet's sums and the import's figure is already whole. The header's total then reads what the book's total reads: `formatMoney` settles before it rounds, so the store's sum in the API's order and the book's in name order show the same dollars (`src/utils/load.js:55-61`; the same reason the Partnership tab agrees with the book). The sums themselves do not change.
- **(ae), U31 (a): one caption and the x ticks.** The caption: "Each point is a client: its <reporting year> revenue against its strategic value, coloured by its first practice area." (the year as every revenue label gives it, D4); the x axis's `unit` replaced by `tickFormatter={formatMoney}`, so the ticks read "$80,000". No new axis, no size, no change to the data or the tooltip beyond (ad).
- **Nothing else moves:** no route is added (the inventory stays at 29), no schema, no feature name (the page's three changes read what every API sends; the import's one line changes no response), no write, no dependency; `utils/book.cjs`, the scorer, P10 and T5 untouched, and `GET /api/ai/book` byte for byte as before (15.6 checks it).

### 15.3 What a partner sees

Under U27 to U31 as recommended, on 13.4's synthetic book (amounts with cents):

- The header's total and the Dashboard's Total Revenue read `$540,752`, as the Partnership tab's total and the AI tab's book do, in place of `$540,751.5`; a client with $12,389.70 reads `$12,390` on its card, in the top-clients table, in the chart tooltips and in Stage 1, as it does in the lead sheet. After a Check file or an import: `years 2025 ($235,000.00), 2026 ($290,000.50)`.
- The Analysis sub-tab's chart with x ticks `$0`, `$80,000`, `$160,000`, `$310,000` and, under it, "Each point is a client: its 2026 revenue against its strategic value, coloured by its first practice area."
- Nothing from (aa), (ab) or (ac): an import that meets a rename completes instead of failing once, the page's styles are the same bytes, and the runbook gains a paragraph.

### 15.4 What the scratch runs found

On 2026-10-01, scratch scripts in the session's scratchpad, never committed, against `4041df1` and a scratch worktree of it.

- **(aa) reproduced, 3 of 3.** On production's older tables (integer ids), a `server.cjs` on a throwaway database, an account, and per round two clients led by Kevin, Alder (the lower id) inserted first, then Birch, then Alder's row updated, so that a read without `ORDER BY` returned `[Birch, Alder]` (checked each round); the holder, a connection of the script's own with `deadlock_timeout` 10 minutes as WP2's `lockHolder` (`tests/routes.test.mjs:505-512`), running the rename statement by statement: Kevin `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, then Alder `FOR UPDATE` (the first of the rename's `ORDER BY id` locks); the import, with no `Lead` column, naming both. Once the import waited (`pg_stat_activity`), Birch `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT` on the holder answered `55P03`: the import held Birch while waiting for Alder. The holder's `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` of Birch then waited about 980 ms, the import's backend's `deadlock_timeout`, and the import answered 500 `Failed to process CSV data`, its server logging `Error processing a CSV import: {"code":"40P01","message":"deadlock detected"}`, nothing written; the holder's lock was granted. 3 of 3 rounds. **With a `Lead` column**, the same round: the import waited at the People list, held neither client (`NOWAIT` granted), and answered 200 once the holder rolled back, in 41 ms.
- **The one line closes it, 3 of 3.** In the worktree, `ORDER BY id` added at `data.cjs:227` (the diff one insertion); `EXPLAIN` of the read: `LockRows` over `Sort` (`Sort Key: id`) over `Seq Scan on clients`, so the rows are locked sorted. The same three rounds: the import held nothing while it waited (`NOWAIT` granted), the holder's second lock was immediate (0 to 1 ms), and the import answered 200 in 38 to 49 ms with no error logged; the `Lead` round the same.
- **(ab) on PostgreSQL 16.14**, a throwaway database, a table with a `CHECK` and a `NOT NULL`, a parameterized insert of a note under each verbosity: `default` logged `ERROR`, `DETAIL: Failing row contains (1, a default secret note, zzz).` and `STATEMENT: INSERT INTO t (note, kind) VALUES ($1, $2)`; `terse` logged `ERROR` and `STATEMENT` only; `verbose` added the SQLSTATE and `LOCATION: ExecConstraints, execMain.c:2109`; in all three the client received `detail` with the row. A role that is not a superuser (`rolsuper` false) got `permission denied to set parameter "log_error_verbosity"` from `SET`, `ALTER DATABASE ... SET` and `ALTER ROLE ... SET`, and the same for `log_min_error_statement`; after the superuser's `GRANT SET ON PARAMETER log_error_verbosity TO` that role, all three succeeded (`SHOW` answering `terse`). `pg_settings` gives both parameters the context `superuser`.
- **(ac) by the bundle.** In the worktree with `src/App.css:29-118` deleted (the file then `:root`, a blank line, `*`, `body`), `build:prod` wrote `index-CAFf2zvd.css` (39.08 kB) and `index-CNs0yfvx.js` (1,013.88 kB), `cmp` finding the CSS identical to `4041df1`'s and the names the same, with no CSS or config warning; `4041df1`'s CSS holds `prefers-color-scheme:dark` (the `dark:` utilities) and no `.dark{`, `oklch`, `--chart-` or `--sidebar`.

### 15.7 Acceptance

The gates green: lint 0 errors and the 3 warnings; `npm test` 533 (no pure test); `build:prod` with no CSS or config warning, the CSS byte for byte `4041df1`'s and the JS a few hundred bytes different (the caption, the formatters); `npm audit` 0 and 0; `npm ci` 544 and 244. The four database suites at the baselines but `routes` up by its new tests (67, 72, 356 plus them with 29 routes, 33). 15.6's failing-first run and end-to-end run. `data.cjs`'s diff one line and a comment; `utils/book.cjs`, `server.cjs`, `init-db.sql` and `routes/` untouched.

### 15.8 Traps

1. **Locks follow the sort only because `LockRows` sits above `Sort`** (15.4's `EXPLAIN`); write the `ORDER BY` in the same statement as the `FOR UPDATE`, as the rename does. Never cast the id (constraint 1): `ORDER BY id` orders integers on production and uuids on `init-db.sql`'s tables, and the rename sorts the same column, so both agree on either shape.
2. **The test must set the trap and check it.** The scan order is the heap's; on a fresh table without the update the scan meets the lower id first, no cycle forms, and the test passes on `origin/main` too, proving nothing. Update the lower id's row after inserting the higher's, then assert the order of a read without `ORDER BY` before the round.
3. **Timing.** The import's backend detects the deadlock after its own `deadlock_timeout` (the server's default, 1 s) if the cycle exists by then; the holder's second lock must follow the import's wait within that second (`waitFor` polls every 25 ms; 15.4's rounds resolved in about 980 ms). The holder's 10 minutes keep it from being the one aborted. The same timing WP2's (b) and (s) tests rely on.
4. **Check file takes the same locks** and is covered by the same line; a `dryRun` round in the test is optional.
5. **The braces.** Delete `:29-118` whole: the three rules nest inside `@layer base` (`:5-127`), `:52`'s brace opens at the margin and `:84`'s closes indented, and a half deletion unbalances the layer, which Lightning CSS refuses (`[lightningcss minify]`, section 12.8). Keep `:120-126`.
6. **Which figures round.** `formatMoney` is for a revenue figure of the book; the file's year totals stay to the cent, since runbook 7.4 compares them with the sheet's `=SUM`, and say `en-US`.
7. **The sums do not move**, only their writing: the header still sums `getTotalRevenue()` in the API's order, and `formatMoney`'s settling is what makes it read as the book's; do not replace it with the exposure model's total (13.8 trap 2 the other way round: that total has one job).
8. **The caption names the year** (D4), as every revenue label does; in January before the new sheet it is last year's.
9. **Lint.** `no-unused-vars` is an error: `formatCurrency` goes with its one use, and each file that gains `formatMoney` imports it once; the three `exhaustive-deps` warnings stay three.
10. **Nothing in WP10 changes the book's text** (constraint 10): checked byte for byte, as 13.6 and 14.6 did.

### 15.9 Mixed versions and rollback

The page's three ((ac), (ad), (ae)) read what every API sends and need nothing from Render; the import's one line changes no response, so no feature name (constraint 5). Netlify publishes the page about a minute after the merge; Render redeploys the API. Either can be rolled back alone: the page back to `toLocaleString()` and the old caption, the API back to the scan-order lock; nothing is stored, so nothing needs undoing. (ab) is a runbook paragraph.

### 15.10 Jeff, after deploy

On gbacpod.com, after a reload: the header's total and the Dashboard's Total Revenue in whole dollars, equal to the Partnership tab's "Revenue <year>" total and to the AI tab's book; a client card's revenue against its row in the lead sheet; the Analysis sub-tab's x ticks and caption; Data Upload's **Check file** on the downloaded book sheet answering its year totals to the cent (nothing written). `/api/health` `OK`, its features unchanged (twelve). For (ab), once, connected to the production database as runbook 7.1 or 7.2 connects: `SELECT rolsuper FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = current_user;` and `SHOW log_error_verbosity;`, and whether the Render dashboard shows the database's own log lines; the next session records the answers in runbook 9.1. If no client was edited between the deploy and the check, an answer saved before the deploy does not read "on an earlier book", and takes a follow-up.
