## 16. WP7: Second-chair relief for the firm

Written 2026-10-01 against `88a6e7b` (`main` after PR #73), at Jeff's request, as the plan to build from when the trigger comes: section 9's sketch expanded to the shape of sections 13 to 15, with U33 and U34 proposed (section 1). WP7's status stays "not planned" (U10 (a)) until Jeff approves U33 and its trigger has come. No code, test or schema changed for this section: every measurement in 16.4 ran on scratch copies of the page's models and the book's port in the session's scratchpad, never committed. Placed after section 15 so that sections 10 and 11 keep their numbers.

**Whether and when (U33, proposed): build when the trigger comes, not now.** The trigger, as Jeff can see it: the partners have modelled a hire in Scenarios with "Associate relief" on (16.5's check counts the saved hire scenarios that have it on), and then say they want the Partnership tab, the lead sheets, the exports, the rankings and the AI's book to read every load that way. Jeff, 2026-10-01: no hire has been workshopped with the toggle, and no departure or hire is foreseeable; so WP7 is not expected to run in Tier 3, and section 11 carries it. Until then the cheaper alternative is the one already built: S19's toggle shows the relieved reading inside a hire scenario, beside P10's, and changes nothing else. Building now, under U10 (c), would move almost every partner's lead effort, about one departure proposal in six at the firm's shape, and the text of every book, for a reading the partners have not asked for (16.4). Retiring WP7 would save nothing: the toggle stays either way, and a written section costs nothing to keep. **Size:** 1 to 1.5 sessions (section 9 said one; 16.4 found the ledger change and fourteen tests in eight suites). **Risk:** medium. Both sides of the load model, four ranking engines and the book's text change together; a one-sided change fails `tests/book.test.mjs` (16.4), so the risk is in the rankings, which 16.7 pins. **Needs:** U33 (a) with its trigger come; U10's option ((c), as U10 already chose over (b)); U34. **Gate:** 0.2's only: no schema, route, write or feature name, so no backup gate.

### 16.1 What the code has

Checked at `88a6e7b`.

1. **P10's two numbers, on both sides.** `SECOND_CHAIR_EFFORT_SHARE` is 0.2 (`src/utils/load.js:28`) and `secondChairEffort` the share of a client's effort (`:31`). `partnershipModel` (`:153-226`) adds each client's full effort to its lead (`:172`) and the share to its second chair (`:175`), then averages over the active people in each role (`:180-189`) and gives each person's ratios (`:191-209`). The server's port, `utils/book.cjs`, has its own constant (`:37`), adds the same over the sorted entries (`:275-283`), averages the same way (`:287-296`) and adds revenue per effort, revenue led over lead effort (`:318`). The book writes P10 in words: the legend's load sentence, "a client's lead carries its full effort and its second chair 20% of it" (`:427`), and the People tables' headers (`:442-443`). `tests/book.test.mjs` holds the two sides equal (`:226`, `:258-259`, the 200 random books).
2. **Who reads the model.** The Partnership tab (`src/PartnershipAnalytics.jsx:26`, `:38`) and its tables (`src/components/LoadTable.jsx:9`); the lead sheet, opened from the Partnership tab and from the Dashboard's Exposure sub-tab (`src/DashboardView.jsx:114-118`; `src/components/PersonLoadSheet.jsx:153`), whose lead table shows each client's full effort (`:38`, `:45`) and whose second-chair table shows the share (`:180-181`), "so the column adds up to the figure above it" (`:40-43`); the exports (`src/utils/partnershipExports.js:56-80`, the load CSV; `:101-107`, the report's client tables); the load tables of Stage 1 (`src/components/succession/ImpactAnalysisWorkbench.jsx:241`, `:272-275`), the associate split (`src/components/AssociateSplit.jsx:67`, `:147`), the hire view (`src/components/succession/HireScenario.jsx:284-335`) and the sandbox (`src/components/succession/ClientSandbox.jsx:39`, `:61`, `:293-306`); and the transition plan's roster: the page's `rosterFor` (`src/utils/transitionPlans.js:21-27`), which the server ignores (T14), and the server's `rosterFromBook` (`utils/transitionPlan.cjs:149-156`), written into each plan's roster lines (`:125-128`).
3. **The rankings, on a ledger of single seats.** `totalLoad` (`src/utils/departure.js:57-64`) is lead effort plus second-chair effort, and `rankCandidates` ranks on it after practice-area fit (`:133-151`, `:148`). `createLedger` (`:70-89`) adds or removes one seat at a time, a lead's at the client's full effort and a second chair's at the share (`:81`), so a lead's load never depends on the other seat. Four models build one: the departure engine (`:233-239`, settled at `:322-332`), the associate split (`src/utils/associateSplit.js:56-62`, `:98`), the hire scenario (`src/utils/hireScenario.js:236-245`, `:265-276`) and the sandbox (`src/utils/clientFit.js:272-278`).
4. **S19 and U9's device.** `RELIEVED_LEAD_SHARE`, 1 − the share (`src/utils/hireScenario.js:67`); `reliefFigures` (`:103-128`), computed only with the toggle on (`:386-391`) and shown in two columns beside P10's (`HireScenario.jsx:294`, `:305-306`, `:327-332`; the checkbox `:419-421`); the store's `relief` (`src/portfolioStore.js:88`, `:854-855`); the saved state's `relief` on both sides (`utils/scenarioState.cjs:84`, `:373`; `src/utils/scenarioState.js:58`, `:114`, `:367`, `:571`). U9's device (`hireScenario.js:243-245`, `:272`) counts each settled seat as relief of its lead in the ranking alone, whatever the toggle, so proposals spread across the heavy partners (U9 (a)).
5. **The tests that pin P10 today.** 16.4 ran the suite against each option: these fail, and are the ones WP7 rewrites: `tests/load.test.mjs:25`; `tests/book.test.mjs:226` and `:608`; `tests/departure.test.mjs:109`, `:160` and `:186`; `tests/hire-scenario.test.mjs:74` and `:87`; `tests/client-fit.test.mjs:263`, `:353` and `:531`; `tests/partnership-exports.test.mjs:37`; `tests/transition-plan.test.mjs:253`; and, under (c) only, `tests/transition-plans.test.mjs:45`. The relief tests of the hire suite (`tests/hire-scenario.test.mjs:213`, `:225`, `:368`) and the state's (`tests/scenario-state.test.mjs:415-500`) pass under either option and change with U34.

### 16.2 The design, and how the page and server copies stay equal

Under U10 (c), the option U10 already prefers; (b) differs only where noted.

1. **The rule.** A client's lead carries `1 − SECOND_CHAIR_EFFORT_SHARE` of its effort (0.8) when the client has a second chair, and all of it otherwise; the second chair carries the share, as now. Counts and revenue count in full for both chairs, as now. A second chair who is inactive but still holds the seat relieves the lead: the seat is held. Each client's own effort, its strategic value, its succession metrics and the exposure do not change. Under (b) only a second chair whose role on the People list is associate relieves the lead (the role read from `rowFor`'s person, the People list's, not the nested copy).
2. **The page.** `src/utils/load.js` gains `LEAD_SHARE_WITH_SECOND_CHAIR` and `leadEffort(effort, secondChair)` beside `secondChairEffort` (`:28-31`), and `partnershipModel` adds `leadEffort(...)` at `:172`. `RELIEVED_LEAD_SHARE` (`hireScenario.js:67`) goes; its readers read the new constant.
3. **The server.** `utils/book.cjs` the same at `:279`, its constant beside `:37` with a comment naming the page's; the legend's load sentence (`:427`) states the rule. The People tables' headers stay (`Lead effort`; `Second-chair effort (20%)`).
4. **A ledger that settles a client's two seats together.** Under relief a lead's load depends on the other seat, so `createLedger` gains `seat(clientId, { lead, second, revenue, effort })`, which takes the client's previous pair off and puts the new pair on. A second chair who leaves raises their lead's load back to the full effort until the seat is filled, and a seat the engine cannot fill leaves it there. The four engines call `seat` (the departure engine at `:233-239` and `:322-332`, the split at `:56-62` and `:98`, the hire scenario at `:236-245` and `:268-271`, the sandbox at `:272-278`), and `add` and `remove` go with their last callers. `totalLoad` and `rankCandidates` are unchanged. Under P10 the scratch ledger gave every candidate, proposal and seat the repository's code gives, on all 601 books of 16.4.
5. **The hire scenario (U34 (a)).** U9's device (`hireScenario.js:243-245`, `:272`) goes: the relief it imitated is now real, and keeping it would count each seat's relief twice. S19's toggle, its two columns and `reliefFigures` go. `relief` stays in the state on both sides, accepted and saved back as it was loaded, so saved scenarios open, a page not yet reloaded still saves, and a rollback's page finds its toggle as it was.
6. **Columns that add up.** The lead sheet's lead table (`PersonLoadSheet.jsx:38`, `:45`) and the report's (`partnershipExports.js:101-107`) show each client's effort as the lead carries it, headed "Effort (80% with a second chair)". The load CSV writes the model's lead effort (`:74`) and needs no change.
7. **The words.** Stage 1's sentence (`ImpactAnalysisWorkbench.jsx:241`), the sandbox's comment and line (`clientFit.js:38-41`, `ClientSandbox.jsx:61`) and the hire view's line say the rule; `docs/plans/people-and-second-chair.md`'s P10 gains a second amendment, with Jeff's words and date, and `CLAUDE.md`'s P10 sentences change with it.
8. **Revenue per effort** (the book only: `utils/book.cjs:318`, `:429`, `:491`) stays revenue led over lead effort, so a relieved lead's figure rises, by at most a quarter (1 ÷ 0.8); the legend's sentence says lead effort is after relief. The page shows no revenue per effort.
9. **How the two sides stay equal.** By the parity suite as it is: `tests/book.test.mjs` compares every person's lead effort to within 1e-9 on the fixture book and 200 random books, and a copy with only the page's side changed fails 6 of its 23 tests (16.4). WP7 adds a test that states the rule on a book worked by hand, on both sides.
10. **Not changed:** `SECOND_CHAIR_EFFORT_SHARE`; `getEffort` and the scorer; the succession metrics (they read the client's effort, not loads); exposure and T5; the role averages' membership; P3 and P9; the transition plan's prompt, whose roster lines already read the book's rows (T14) and so show the new lead effort; any route, the schema and `/api/health`.

### 16.3 What the partners would see

On the fixture book (`tests/fixtures/books.mjs`), reporting year 2026, lead effort with its ratio to the partners' average:

| Partner | P10 (a) | U10 (b) | U10 (c) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Kevin (seconded by Jay and by Anna) | 7.5 (3.6×) | 6.6 (3.5×) | 6 (3.5×) |
| Paula (one client seconded by Anna) | 3 (1.4×) | 2.6 (1.4×) | 2.6 (1.5×) |
| Brendan (seconded by Kevin) | 2 (1.0×) | 2 (1.1×) | 1.6 (0.9×) |
| The partners' average | 2.08 | 1.87 | 1.70 |

Brendan's ratio under (b) is the effect that surprises: his load does not move, the average falls, so his ratio rises. The Partnership tab, the lead sheets (from it and from the Dashboard's Exposure sub-tab), the exports, Stage 1's and the sandbox's load tables and the AI's People tables all show the right-hand figures; the hire view shows them without a toggle. A departure's proposals, the hire scenario's seats and the sandbox's proposal can change where loads decide (16.4); the associate split's proposals do not.

### 16.4 What the measurements found

On 2026-10-01, scratch scripts in the session's scratchpad, never committed: copies of `src/utils` and `utils/book.cjs` with the lead's share switchable between P10 as amended, U10 (b) and U10 (c), and the ledger of 16.2 item 4. Under P10 the copies gave the repository's candidates, proposals and seats on every book below (the departure engine with each active person leaving, the associate split, the hire scenario), so every difference is the option's. Three families of books: the fixture book; 300 random books from `tests/book.test.mjs`'s generator (seeds 1 to 300: 2 to 12 people of any role, some inactive, 0 to 40 clients, about 30% without a second chair and 10% without a lead); and 300 firm-shaped books (the six partners, the emeritus and one or two associates, 84 clients, production's count on 2026-09-29, each led by an active partner, a share of 0 to 100% of them with a second chair of any role, every cadence, half unrated). The departure engine ran with each active person leaving alone; the hire scenario with one hypothetical associate at its default target and no focus; the sandbox with a $100,000 Healthcare client at Monthly, not rated.

| Firm-shaped books (300) | U10 (b) | U10 (c) |
|---|---|---|
| Active partners whose lead effort moves (of 1,800) | 1,150 | 1,671 |
| ... whose lead-effort ratio reads differently | 220 | 384 |
| Books whose partners rank differently by lead effort | 67 | 107 |
| Largest fall in one partner's lead effort | 7 (13.9%) | 11.9 (20%) |
| Departure decisions with a different proposal (of 37,560, in 2,487 scenarios) | 4,736 (12.6%), in 1,185 scenarios | 6,532 (17.4%), in 1,425 |
| ... of which the lead differs | 4,404 | 5,993 |
| Associate split proposals that differ (of 12,840) | 0 | 0 |
| Hire scenarios whose seats differ (of 284) | 38 | 56 |
| Sandbox proposals that differ (of 300) | 11 | 17 |
| Books whose text differs, figures only (of 300) | 284 | 296 |

On the 300 random books: 346 and 919 of 1,211 active partners' lead effort moves; 147 (2.0%) and 230 (3.1%) of 7,472 departure decisions differ; 0 of 1,563 split proposals; 6 and 19 of 258 hire scenarios; 5 and 10 of 296 sandbox proposals; 181 and 285 of 300 books' figures. The fixture book: Kevin, Paula and Brendan as 16.3, and no departure, split or sandbox proposal differs.

- **Where it bites.** By the share of clients with a second chair, the departure decisions that differ on the firm-shaped books: 0 to 25%, 19% under (b) and 32% under (c) (of 7,956); 25 to 50%, 17% and 23% (of 8,386); 50 to 75%, 11% and 14% (of 10,391); 75 to 100%, 5% and 6% (of 10,827). Relief reorders most when few clients have a second chair: it lightens a few partners and not the rest. When nearly every client has one, (c) lightens every lead by about a fifth and the order holds. Production's coverage is unknown to a session; 16.5's check prints it.
- **The split never moves**, by construction: it proposes the best-ranked associate, ranked on associates' loads, and associates lead nothing. The leads' figures after the split's proposals do fall.
- **The book's text changes for every book**, whatever its figures: the legend's load sentence (`utils/book.cjs:427`) states P10. On the deploy every WP10 thread stops taking follow-ups (409) and every saved answer reads "on an earlier book" (constraint 10), as any client edit already does, and the next Ask or plan writes the book to the cache again.
- **The tests.** The suite at `88a6e7b` is 538 tests. With the page's models and the book's port both under the option: 13 fail under (b), in seven suites, and 14 under (c), in eight (16.1 item 5). With only the page's side changed (c): 6 of `tests/book.test.mjs`'s 23 fail (its three parity tests and three exposure tests, each of which compares both sides on the same books), so the two sides cannot drift apart unnoticed. `tests/associate-split.test.mjs` passes under both options, as the split's proposals do not move.

### 16.5 The read-only check, for both packages

For Jeff, from his machine, never in this session. One `node -e` command through `db.cjs`, every read in one REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY transaction, printing counts only: no client, person or account name, no answer's text, no id. It reads `scenarios`, `ai_answers` (the transition plans' text, to compare the recommended names, which it never prints) and `clients` with their second chairs' roles. Tested on 2026-10-01 in the container on both table shapes (`init-db.sql`'s and production's older tables with integer ids, `PRODUCTION_TABLES_SQL`), each seeded with hire and departure scenarios, saved plans and clients, every count checked by hand, and the transaction refusing a write (`25006`). The script holds no `$`, backtick or double quote, so PowerShell passes it unchanged.

```powershell
Set-Location <path to your clone of client-portfolio>
git pull; npm ci
$env:DATABASE_URL = Read-Host 'External Database URL' -MaskInput
$env:DATABASE_SSL = 'no-verify'
$check = @'
const db = require('./db.cjs');
const { getEffort } = require('./utils/strategic.cjs');
const line = (text, heading) => {
  const at = String(text || '').indexOf('## ' + heading);
  if (at < 0) return null;
  const rest = String(text).slice(at + heading.length + 3).split(/\r?\n/).slice(1).map((l) => l.trim());
  const first = rest.find(Boolean);
  if (!first || first.startsWith('##')) return null;
  return first.replace(/[*_\x60#>\[\]]/g, ' ').replace(/^[\s\-•\d.)]+/, '').replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim().toLowerCase();
};
const tally = (values) => {
  const seen = new Map();
  for (const v of values) seen.set(v, (seen.get(v) || 0) + 1);
  return { distinct: seen.size, most: Math.max(0, ...seen.values()) };
};
const round = (n) => Math.round(n * 100) / 100;
(async () => {
  const c = await db.pool.connect();
  let scenarios, answers, clients;
  try {
    await c.query('BEGIN TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY');
    scenarios = (await c.query('SELECT state FROM scenarios ORDER BY id')).rows.map((r) => r.state || {});
    answers = (await c.query('SELECT kind, answer, book_sha256, input_tokens, output_tokens, cache_read_tokens, cache_write_tokens, cost_usd FROM ai_answers ORDER BY id')).rows.filter((r) => r.kind === 'transition-plan');
    clients = (await c.query('SELECT c.lead_id, c.interaction_frequency, c.high_maintenance, s.role AS second_role FROM clients c LEFT JOIN people s ON s.id = c.second_chair_id')).rows;
    await c.query('COMMIT');
  } finally {
    c.release();
    await db.pool.end();
  }

  const hire = scenarios.filter((s) => s.kind === 'hire');
  console.log('Hire scenarios: ' + hire.length + '; with Associate relief on: ' + hire.filter((s) => s.relief === true).length +
    '; with an associate linked to a person: ' + hire.filter((s) => (s.associates || []).some((a) => a && a.personId)).length);

  const departures = scenarios.filter((s) => s.kind !== 'hire');
  const aiPlans = (s) => Object.entries(s.plans || {}).filter(([, p]) => p && p.ai && p.ai.strategy);
  const withTwo = departures.filter((s) => aiPlans(s).length >= 2);
  console.log('Departure scenarios: ' + departures.length + '; with two or more AI plans: ' + withTwo.length);
  withTwo.forEach((s, i) => {
    const plans = aiPlans(s).map(([, p]) => p.ai);
    const leads = plans.map((ai) => ai.recommendedLead && ai.recommendedLead.person ? String(ai.recommendedLead.person.id) : null).filter((v) => v !== null);
    const seconds = plans.map((ai) => ai.recommendedSecondChair && ai.recommendedSecondChair.person ? String(ai.recommendedSecondChair.person.id) : null).filter((v) => v !== null);
    const l = tally(leads);
    const sc = tally(seconds);
    console.log('  Scenario ' + (i + 1) + ': ' + plans.length + ' AI plans; recommended leads ' + leads.length + ' resolved, ' + l.distinct +
      ' distinct, ' + l.most + ' on the most named; second chairs ' + seconds.length + ' resolved, ' + sc.distinct + ' distinct, ' + sc.most + ' on the most named');
  });

  let rec = 0, same = 0, sameApproved = 0, sameOpen = 0, recSecond = 0, sameSecond = 0;
  for (const s of departures) {
    for (const [id, p] of aiPlans(s)) {
      const choice = (s.choices || {})[id] || {};
      const lead = p.ai.recommendedLead && p.ai.recommendedLead.person;
      if (lead) {
        rec += 1;
        if (choice.leadId !== undefined && String(choice.leadId) === String(lead.id)) {
          same += 1;
          if (p.status === 'approved') sameApproved += 1; else sameOpen += 1;
        }
      }
      const second = p.ai.recommendedSecondChair;
      if (second && (second.person || second.none)) {
        recSecond += 1;
        const want = second.person ? String(second.person.id) : null;
        if (choice.secondChairId !== undefined && (choice.secondChairId === null ? null : String(choice.secondChairId)) === want) sameSecond += 1;
      }
    }
  }
  console.log('Saved choices equal to the AI recommended lead: ' + same + ' of ' + rec + ' plans with one (' + sameApproved + ' on approved plans, ' + sameOpen +
    ' on plans not approved); to its second chair: ' + sameSecond + ' of ' + recSecond);

  const byBook = new Map();
  for (const a of answers) {
    const k = a.book_sha256 || 'none';
    if (!byBook.has(k)) byBook.set(k, []);
    byBook.get(k).push(a);
  }
  const runs = [...byBook.values()].filter((g) => g.length >= 2);
  console.log('Saved transition plans: ' + answers.length + ', on ' + byBook.size + ' books; books with two or more plans: ' + runs.length);
  const mean = (pick) => (answers.length ? Math.round(answers.reduce((sum, a) => sum + (Number(pick(a)) || 0), 0) / answers.length) : 0);
  const priced = answers.filter((a) => a.cost_usd !== null);
  console.log('  Per plan, on average: input ' + mean((a) => a.input_tokens) + ', cache read ' + mean((a) => a.cache_read_tokens) + ', cache write ' + mean((a) => a.cache_write_tokens) +
    ', output ' + mean((a) => a.output_tokens) + ' tokens; cost ' + (priced.length ? (priced.reduce((sum, a) => sum + Number(a.cost_usd), 0) / priced.length).toFixed(4) : 'not priced') + ' USD');
  runs.forEach((g, i) => {
    const leads = g.map((a) => line(a.answer, 'RECOMMENDED LEAD')).filter(Boolean);
    const seconds = g.map((a) => line(a.answer, 'RECOMMENDED SECOND CHAIR')).filter(Boolean);
    const l = tally(leads);
    const sc = tally(seconds);
    console.log('  Book ' + (i + 1) + ': ' + g.length + ' plans; lead lines ' + leads.length + ', ' + l.distinct + ' distinct, ' + l.most +
      ' on the most named; second-chair lines ' + seconds.length + ', ' + sc.distinct + ' distinct, ' + sc.most + ' on the most named');
  });

  const led = clients.filter((r) => r.lead_id !== null);
  const seconded = led.filter((r) => r.second_role);
  const byRole = (role) => seconded.filter((r) => r.second_role === role);
  const moved = (rows) => round(rows.reduce((sum, r) => sum + 0.2 * getEffort(r), 0));
  const leads = (rows) => new Set(rows.map((r) => String(r.lead_id))).size;
  console.log('Clients: ' + clients.length + '; with a lead: ' + led.length + '; with a second chair: ' + seconded.length + ' (partner ' + byRole('partner').length +
    ', emeritus ' + byRole('emeritus').length + ', associate ' + byRole('associate').length + ')');
  console.log('Lead effort in the book: ' + round(led.reduce((sum, r) => sum + getEffort(r), 0)) + '; relief under U10 (b) would move ' + moved(byRole('associate')) +
    ' of it, over ' + leads(byRole('associate')) + ' leads; under U10 (c) ' + moved(seconded) + ', over ' + leads(seconded) + ' leads');
})().catch((error) => {
  console.error('Check failed: ' + (error.code || '') + ' ' + error.message);
  process.exit(1);
});
'@
node -e $check
Remove-Item Env:DATABASE_URL, Env:DATABASE_SSL
```

If PowerShell ever mangles the argument, `$check | node -` runs the same script from the standard input, from the same directory. In Windows PowerShell 5.1, drop `-MaskInput` (runbook conventions). What it printed on the container's seeded test database (synthetic data; the shape of the answer, not production's):

```
Hire scenarios: 2; with Associate relief on: 1; with an associate linked to a person: 1
Departure scenarios: 2; with two or more AI plans: 1
  Scenario 1: 4 AI plans; recommended leads 4 resolved, 2 distinct, 3 on the most named; second chairs 3 resolved, 2 distinct, 2 on the most named
Saved choices equal to the AI recommended lead: 2 of 5 plans with one (1 on approved plans, 1 on plans not approved); to its second chair: 1 of 5
Saved transition plans: 5, on 2 books; books with two or more plans: 1
  Per plan, on average: input 1200, cache read 7000, cache write 0, output 2500 tokens; cost 0.0321 USD
  Book 1: 4 plans; lead lines 4, 2 distinct, 3 on the most named; second-chair lines 4, 3 distinct, 2 on the most named
Clients: 6; with a lead: 5; with a second chair: 4 (partner 1, emeritus 1, associate 2)
Lead effort in the book: 15.5; relief under U10 (b) would move 1.3 of it, over 2 leads; under U10 (c) 2.1, over 3 leads
```

How to read it:

- **WP7's trigger, half of it:** "with Associate relief on" above 0 means a hire has been modelled with the toggle on and saved. The other half is the partners saying they want it firm-wide, which no query shows.
- **WP7's size on production:** the last two lines. The clients with a second chair, by its role, and the lead effort relief would move under each option: the larger the share of clients with a second chair, the less the rankings move (16.4).
- **WP8's problem:** each saved departure scenario with two or more AI plans gives how many plans named one lead ("3 on the most named" of 4 plans is three on one person); the saved plans grouped by the book they were written on give the same for plans whose scenario was never saved (likely Tier 1 WP6's four among them). Plans of one run share the book they were written on; two runs share one only when no client was edited between them, so a group can hold two runs.
- **"Use these picks":** a saved choice equal to the AI's recommended lead on a plan not approved is the likeliest sign of it (a partner could also have picked that person by hand). Approval pins every seat as a choice (`pinnedChoice`, `src/utils/transitionPlans.js:48-52`), so on an approved plan an equal choice may only mean the engine and the AI agreed.
- **WP8's cost:** the per-plan average of the saved plans' tokens and cost, for 17.5's estimate.

### 16.6 Expected outcomes

When built, under U10 (c) and U34 (a):

- `src/utils/load.js`: `LEAD_SHARE_WITH_SECOND_CHAIR` and `leadEffort`; `partnershipModel` using it. `utils/book.cjs`: the same, and the legend's load sentence.
- `src/utils/departure.js`: `createLedger().seat`, and the four engines settling a client's two seats together; `add` and `remove` gone.
- `src/utils/hireScenario.js`: U9's device, `RELIEVED_LEAD_SHARE`, `reliefFigures` and the toggle's figures gone; `HireScenario.jsx` without the checkbox and its two columns; the store and the state keeping `relief` as loaded.
- `PersonLoadSheet.jsx` and `partnershipExports.js`: the lead tables' effort column as the lead carries it.
- The words of 16.2 item 7; P10's second amendment in `docs/plans/people-and-second-chair.md`; `CLAUDE.md`.
- The fourteen tests of 16.1 item 5 rewritten to the rule, and 16.7's new ones.
- No route, schema, feature name, write or dependency; the scorer, the exposure and every client's own figures unchanged.

**Not in this package:** a share other than 20% (P10's, Jeff's call); a capacity ceiling; relief that depends on who the second chair is beyond U10's option; any change to the transition plan's prompt beyond what the book's rows carry.

### 16.7 Tests

Each new or changed test is shown failing first against `origin/main`'s code, in a worktree with only the test files copied in (0.2 item 6): the code under test is the change, so the old code fails each one on the rule.

- `tests/load.test.mjs`: the rule on a hand-worked book: a lead with a second chair carries 80% of that client's effort and 100% of a client without one; an inactive second chair still relieves; counts and revenue whole for both chairs; the partners' average and a ratio that rises because the average fell (16.3's Brendan under (b)).
- `tests/book.test.mjs`: the same figures in the book's People rows; the legend's sentence; `LEAD_SHARE_WITH_SECOND_CHAIR` equal on both sides; the parity tests unchanged (they bind the sides).
- `tests/departure.test.mjs`: `seat` replacing a client's pair; a vacated second chair raising its lead to the full effort until filled; a candidate order that relief reverses (two partners whose P10 totals rank one way and whose relieved totals rank the other); every existing test rewritten to the relieved figures.
- `tests/hire-scenario.test.mjs`: proposals still spread across two heavy partners with no device, from real relief; a partner-held seat taken relieving the partner and not the lead (under (c) the lead already carries 80%); no toggle in the model; the relief tests at `:213`, `:225` and `:368` replaced.
- `tests/scenario-state.test.mjs`: a hire state with `relief` true and false checked, opened and saved back unchanged on both sides.
- `tests/client-fit.test.mjs`: the sandbox's lead gaining 80% of the new client's effort with a second chair proposed and 100% with None.
- `tests/partnership-exports.test.mjs`: the report's lead table adding up to the lead effort above it.
- `tests/transition-plan.test.mjs` and `tests/transition-plans.test.mjs`: the roster's lead loads, the book's and the page's, relieved.
- The database suites unchanged: no route changes; `import-db`'s roster parity (the page's loads against the book's) passes with both sides changed.
- End to end, scratch scripts as for every package: a local `server.cjs` on production's older tables, a synthetic book with partner, emeritus and associate second chairs, the Vite dev server, headless Chromium: (1) each partner's lead effort on the Partnership tab equal to their row in `GET /api/ai/book`'s People table, and to the hand sum; (2) the lead sheet's lead table adding up; (3) Stage 1 with a partner leaving, its candidates in the relieved order; (4) the hire view with no toggle, a hire scenario saved with `relief` true by `origin/main`'s page opening and saving with it kept; (5) `GET /api/ai/book` from `origin/main`'s server and the branch's on the same database differing only in the legend's sentence and the lead-effort and revenue-per-effort cells (a diff of the two texts).

### 16.8 Acceptance

The gates green: lint 0 errors and the 3 warnings; `npm test` 538 less the rewritten tests' old forms plus the new; `build:prod` with no CSS or config warning; `npm audit` 0 and 0. The four database suites at the baselines (67, 72, 362 with 29 routes, 33). 16.7's failing-first run and end-to-end run. The book's text diff of 16.7 (5) holding nothing else.

### 16.9 Traps

1. **Both sides in one PR.** A page-only change fails 6 of `book`'s tests (16.4); a server-only one fails the same six. Change `leadEffort` and its constant on both sides together, and the legend with them.
2. **The ledger.** Relief makes a lead's load depend on the other seat: settle a client's two seats together, never one seat alone. A second chair who leaves (or is promoted to lead) gives the lead back the full effort until the engine fills the seat; a seat with no candidate leaves it there. The departure engine's initial ledger leaves out the departing people's seats (`:233-239`), so a client whose second chair leaves starts at the full effort.
3. **U9's device would count twice.** Under relief the lead's load falls for real when a seat is settled; keeping the device subtracts the share again. Remove it, and keep U9's property: the proposals still spread (16.7).
4. **Keep `relief` in the state.** `checkState` refuses a key it does not name (`onlyKeys`, `utils/scenarioState.cjs:232`), so removing `relief` from `HIRE_STATE_KEYS` (`:84`) would refuse every saved hire scenario that holds it and every save from a page not yet reloaded. Keep the key and its boolean check (`:373`) on both sides; the page saves back what it loaded.
5. **Columns that add up.** The lead sheet's lead table shows the client's full effort today (`PersonLoadSheet.jsx:38`), which would no longer add up to the figure above it; the report's the same (`partnershipExports.js:107`).
6. **The book's text changes on the deploy, for every book** (the legend). Every thread stops taking follow-ups and every answer reads "on an earlier book" at once. Say so in the PR; Jeff picks the day.
7. **Ratios move without loads.** The partners' average falls, so a partner whose clients have no second chair rises in ratio with an unchanged load (16.3). The PR names it, and the Partnership tab's figures are read against the new average.
8. **(b) reads the People list's role**, not the nested person's, and an inactive associate who still holds a seat relieves the lead as any seat does.
9. **Rounding.** Relieved efforts are sums of fifths of quarter-units (As-Needed with a handful is 0.75; 0.8 of it is 0.6): `formatEffort` settles before it rounds (`src/utils/load.js:61`), and the book's sums run in name order; the parity tolerance (1e-9) holds, as 16.4's runs found.
10. **The share stays 0.2.** WP7 changes who carries the effort, not the second chair's share; a different share is a separate decision of Jeff's (P10).

### 16.10 Mixed versions and rollback

Nothing the page sends to the API changes, so no feature name (constraint 5). The page and the book change together and both must deploy: Netlify publishes the page about a minute after the merge and Render redeploys the API within minutes; in between, the Partnership tab reads relief and the AI's book does not. Roll both back together (runbook section 5): one alone leaves the page's figures and the book's disagreeing until the other follows. Nothing stored changes: saved hire scenarios keep `relief`; saved answers keep their text, and a plan written before the deploy names P10's loads; the book's hash changes at the deploy and back at a rollback, so a thread begun before the deploy takes follow-ups again after a rollback if no client was edited.

### 16.11 Jeff, after deploy

On gbacpod.com, after a reload: for a partner with clients that have a second chair, the Partnership tab's lead effort equals the sum of 80% of each such client's effort and all of each other's, and equals their row in the AI tab's "What the AI is given"; the lead sheet's lead table adds up to the figure above it; the legend in "What the AI is given" states the rule. Scenarios: a hire scenario has no "Associate relief" checkbox, and one saved with it on opens and saves; Stage 1 with someone leaving lists candidates by the relieved total load. `/api/health` `OK`, its features unchanged (twelve). An answer saved before the deploy reads "on an earlier book" and a thread begun before refuses a follow-up: expected (16.9 trap 6).

---

## 17. WP8: AI plans that see the scenario's seats

Written 2026-10-01 against `88a6e7b` (`main` after PR #73), at Jeff's request, as the plan to build from: section 9's sketch expanded to the shape of sections 13 to 15, with U35 to U39 proposed (section 1). WP8's status stays "not planned" (U11 (a)) until Jeff approves U35. A prototype of the server's half and of the page's request (17.4) ran in a scratch worktree in the session's scratchpad, never committed, against the fake Anthropic server; no code, test or schema changed for this section. Everything about the model, caching and the request comes from the `claude-api` skill (cached 2026-09-25, loaded for this section on 2026-10-01); where it and this plan disagree, the skill wins (0.1 item 3).

**Whether and when (U35 and U36, proposed): WP8 when a real departure becomes known, and Stage 2's note now.** WP8's trigger, as Jeff can see it: a partner, the emeritus or an associate gives notice, or the partners say they will run Scenarios for a real departure; WP8 is then built and deployed before Stage 2 is run for it (one session, inside any notice period). U11 (a)'s trigger, "the partners first rely on the AI's seat picks in a real departure", comes too late for the departure it names: by the time they rely on the picks, the plans have been written alone and the picks applied. Jeff, 2026-10-01: no real departure has used "Use these picks", the partners expect to, and no departure or hire is foreseeable. So building WP8 now spends a session on a change whose effect can be measured only on real answers (17.5), with no departure to measure it on; retiring it would leave the partners taking per-client picks that, in Tier 1 WP6's check, named one lead on four plans of four. The cheaper alternative, now (U36 (a)): one line beside "Use these picks" saying that each plan is written for its client alone and does not see the seats the scenario gives the other clients, and that Stage 1's proposals spread the load; page only, about a fifth of a session, as a package of its own (WP12), reworded by WP8 when it lands (17.12). If 16.5's check shows the saved plans already spread across several leads, the note can wait with WP8. **Size:** WP8 one session (U11 said one to one and a half; 17.4's prototype is most of the server's half); WP12 a fifth. **Risk:** low: one optional request field and its check, one section of the user turn and a feature name; nothing stored, no schema, and the system blocks byte for byte the same (17.4), so T8's cache and T14's trust hold. **Needs:** U35, U37, U38 and U39 for WP8; U36 for WP12. **Gate:** 0.2's only: no schema, so no backup gate.

### 17.1 What the code has

The transition-plan path from the page to the saved answer, checked at `88a6e7b`.

1. **The page's requests.** Stage 2's "Generate plans" (`handleGeneratePlans`, `src/components/succession/ClientReviewInterface.jsx:596-636`) first asks `/api/health` for `transition-plan-roster` and sends nothing without it (`:601-605`; the notice `ROSTER_UNAVAILABLE`, `:71-72`), then posts one request per selected client, two in flight at a time (`:635`; D9), each `planRequest(decision, departure, reportingYear)` (`src/utils/transitionPlans.js:35-46`): the client as the store holds it, `stage1Data` (who is leaving, the revenue at risk, the year) and `rosterFor(departure)` (`:21-27`), every active person staying with the page's loads before the departure. Nothing in it names the other affected clients or the seats the scenario gives them, although the page holds both: each decision's `lead.after` and `secondChair.after`, the partner's choice or else the engine's proposal (`departureModel`, `src/utils/departure.js:217-389`, decisions at `:253-335`).
2. **The route** (`routes/scenarios.cjs:75-144`), behind the AI limiters (`:75`): `checkPlanRequest` (`utils/transitionPlan.cjs:286-295`: a client id and name, `stage1Data` an object) or 400 (`:77-80`); the book (`loadBook`, `:82-89`); the client in it by id as text (`bookClient`, `:90-93`) or 404; `checkRoster` (`:95-98`; `utils/transitionPlan.cjs:74-114`: 1 to 50 active people on the People list, none leaving, each once, with well-formed loads, the names and roles the list's) or 400 with `errors`; then the prompt (`:101`), one `complete()` call with `maxTokens` 16,000 (`:40`, `:103-109`), the save (`:110-119`) and the parse (`:120`).
3. **The prompt** (`createTransitionPlanPrompt`, `utils/transitionPlan.cjs:191-274`): `system` is `systemBlocks(book.text)` (`:273`), Ask's two blocks byte for byte (T8); the user turn holds the date, who is leaving and what is asked (`:212-214`), THE SCENARIO, THE CLIENT from its book entry and its succession analysis (`:225-242`, T14), the ROSTER from the book's rows (`rosterFromBook`, `:149-156`; the lines `:125-128`), each staying person's loads as the book stands, the leaving person's clients still under them, and the seven headings with what each asks (`:248-271`): the lead "from the practice areas they hold, their load against the partners' average and the client's needs" (`:254`).
4. **The saved answer and the parse.** `answerRow` with kind `transition-plan`, the client's id as text and the book's name for it (`:110-119`; `utils/aiAnswers.cjs:66`); the prompt itself is not saved. `parseTransitionPlanResponse` (`utils/transitionPlan.cjs:359-412`) resolves each recommendation against the checked roster (`resolveRecommendation`, `:324-351`), so a name off the roster fills nothing.
5. **"Use these picks".** The page keeps the AI's fields apart from the partner's edits (`withAiPlan`, `src/utils/scenarioState.js:181`; `aiFields`, `:143`); `AiRecommendation` (`ClientReviewInterface.jsx:127-150`) shows "The AI recommends X as lead and Y as second chair" with "Use these picks" (`:146`), which `handleUseAi` (`:661-667`) turns into the partner's choices for that client, still checked by the engine (`assignmentProblems`).
6. **The plan in a saved scenario.** `plans[clientId]` holds `{ answerId, ai, edits, status, updatedAt }` (`utils/scenarioState.cjs:65`), `ai` the route's fields (`:67-70`), each recommendation `{ person, none, text, problem }` (`:72-73`), checked at `:259-300`; a scenario holds at most 500 clients (`:97`).
7. **The feature names** (`server.cjs:153`): twelve, `transition-plan-roster` the one this path reads; `tests/routes.test.mjs:177` lists them.
8. **The cache.** The plan's system blocks are Ask's, so a plan reads the book from the entry a question wrote. The skill: a cache entry becomes readable only once the first response begins streaming, so the two plans sent together both write and the plans after read (CLAUDE.md's "a run shows cache reads from its third plan on").

### 17.2 The design, and how the page and server copies stay equal

1. **The request gains `seats`**, optional: `[{ clientId, leadId, secondChairId }]`, one entry per other affected client of the departure, each the seat the scenario gives it now (`lead.after` and `secondChair.after`), the person ids as text (as the saved state holds them) or null, the client id as the store holds it (an integer on production, a uuid on `init-db.sql`'s tables). The plan's own client is not in it (U38 (a)). `planRequest` gains `{ seats }` (`src/utils/transitionPlans.js:35-46`); without it the body is today's.
2. **The server checks it** with a pure `checkSeats` in `utils/transitionPlan.cjs`, after the roster (`routes/scenarios.cjs:95-98`) and before the prompt. Absent: today's request. Otherwise at most 500 entries (the state's client limit), each a client in the book (by id as text, `bookClient`), not the plan's own, named once, and held now by someone leaving (its book entry's lead or second chair among `departingNames`); a lead that is null (no partner can take it in the scenario) or a partner on the checked roster; a second chair that is null, or on the roster and not the lead; each person id as text. Any problem answers 400 `{ success: false, error, errors }`, as the roster's do, before the model is called, and nothing is saved. The server trusts no figure the page sends (T14): `seats` holds ids alone, and every name, role and seat comes from the book and the People list.
3. **The user turn gains one section** after the ROSTER and before the seven headings (17.3), describing only the seats that change hands: a lead or second chair other than the book's, or a client left without a lead. It is grouped by roster person in the roster's order, each client by the book's name, with no figure (U37 (a)). The lead's instruction (`:254`) adds "the seats the scenario already gives them". Without `seats` the prompt is today's, byte for byte (17.4).
4. **Nothing is added to the system blocks** (T8): the function still returns `systemBlocks(book.text)` (`:273`), so every plan of a run and every Ask on that book share one cache entry, as now.
5. **A feature name**, `transition-plan-seats`, beside `transition-plan-roster` (`server.cjs:153`). The page sends `seats` only when `/api/health` lists it (`ClientReviewInterface.jsx:601-605` already asks before every run): an API without it ignores the field (`checkPlanRequest` reads `client` and `stage1Data`), and would write the plans without the seats while the page said they had them.
6. **What the page says.** WP12's line (17.12) reads, with the feature, that each plan saw the seats the scenario gave the other clients when it was written; without it, WP12's text.
7. **How the copies stay equal.** There is one copy of each half: the page builds `seats` from the departure model (`planRequest`), and the server checks and describes them; `tests/transition-plans.test.mjs` holds the page's half, `tests/transition-plan.test.mjs` the server's, and `tests/routes.test.mjs` the two through the route on both table shapes (17.7).
8. **Unchanged:** the response (`plan`, `answerId`, `saved`; T14), the saved answer's columns, the roster check and `resolveRecommendation`, "Use these picks" (one client at a time, P9), the plan's state in a saved scenario (`PLAN_KEYS`, `AI_FIELDS`), `maxTokens` 16,000 (T11), the rate limits (T16: the same number of requests), two plans at a time (D9), and the output format.
9. **The model.** Production answers on `claude-sonnet-5-5` at `high` (Tier 3 WP1). The skill, for this model: no prefill, no forced tool use and no `thinking: {type: "disabled"}` (each a 400), none of which the service sends; the effort levels are recalibrated from Claude Sonnet 5, and `high` is set explicitly. Structured outputs are available on it, and an enum of the roster's names would make every pick resolvable; `--plans` found all four saved plans' picks resolved (Tier 2 section 16), so WP8 keeps the plan in prose.

### 17.3 What the plan is given

The section as the prototype wrote it for one of Kevin's clients in 17.4's book (tree names, synthetic):

```
## THE SCENARIO'S OTHER SEATS
The scenario on the partners' screen already gives these seats on the other clients the people leaving hold a seat on (the partners' picks, and otherwise the tool's balanced proposals). They are not other plans' recommendations: each plan is written alone. The roster's figures above do not count them, so weigh them with those figures when you recommend, so that no one is handed more than their share.
- Jeff (Partner): would lead Birch Healthcare and Hemlock Healthcare
- Joe (Partner): would lead Ginkgo Education
- Mike (Partner): would lead Elm Municipal and Fir Financial; would be second chair on Juniper Financial
- Paula (Partner): would lead Alder Healthcare and Dogwood Energy; would be second chair on Fir Financial
- Ash (Associate): would be second chair on Alder Healthcare and Pine Healthcare
```

A client left without a lead adds "- Left without a lead in this scenario so far: <names>."; a scenario in which no other seat has changed hands says "- No other client changes hands yet in this scenario." The wording is the prototype's; the PR may tighten it, under 17.9 trap 8. The partners see nothing new in Stage 2 but WP12's line, reworded (17.12).

### 17.4 What the prototype found

On 2026-10-01, scratch scripts in the session's scratchpad and a scratch worktree at `88a6e7b`, never committed. The worktree held 17.2's server half (`checkSeats`, the section, the route's call) and page half (`planRequest`'s `{ seats }`). A throwaway database on production's older tables (integer ids); 16 tree-named clients, Kevin leading 8 of them and seconding 2, across five practice areas and every cadence, with Ash an associate; `origin/main`'s `server.cjs` and the prototype's on that database and one fake Anthropic server (`startFakeAnthropic`). With Kevin leaving (10 affected clients), three requests per client: today's body to today's server, today's body to the prototype, and the body with seats to the prototype; 30 in all.

- **The system blocks: one value across the 30 requests**, byte for byte (13,477 characters), its book block equal to `GET /api/ai/book`'s text and the cache marker on the book block only. Every other field of the request (model, `max_tokens`, the beta, `fallbacks`, `output_config`) the same with and without seats.
- **Today's prompt without seats:** the prototype's user turn equal to today's server's, byte for byte, 10 of 10.
- **With seats:** each turn begins with today's, up to the seven headings, then the section. It adds 871 to 911 characters (mean 896) to a turn of 3,769 on average: about 224 tokens at the book's 4 characters a token (`CHARS_PER_TOKEN`, `utils/book.cjs:40`), 300 at 3. They are uncached (the user turn), at $2 a million input tokens on `claude-sonnet-5-5` about $0.0005 a plan, half a cent for the ten. The size follows the departure, not the book: about 430 characters of preamble, a line for each person who gains a seat and a name for each seat. A real count is one plan's `ai_call` `inputTokens` with and without, after the deploy.
- **The spread the plans now see:** Kevin's seven other led clients went to Jeff, Joe, Mike and Paula, two, one, two and two (the engine's balanced proposals), with Ash and Mike and Paula taking second chairs.
- **Refusals before the model:** seven bodies, each answered 400 with the reason, and none reached the fake: a client not in the book, the plan's own client, a client nobody leaving holds, the person leaving as lead (not on the roster), an associate as lead, a lead as its own second chair, and a person id sent as a number.
- **Nothing moves without seats:** `tests/transition-plan.test.mjs` and `tests/transition-plans.test.mjs` pass on the prototype unchanged (21 of 21).
- **What it cannot show:** whether real plans then spread. The fake recommends the roster's first partner whatever it is sent; that is 17.5's question.

### 17.5 The evaluation (U39)

Recommended, U39 (a): in the app, with no new code. Before WP8 deploys: a departure scenario shaped like a real one (Jeff names who leaves; the partner with the largest lead book is the hardest case), Stage 2's plans for every affected client, saved as "<name>, plans written alone". After WP8 deploys, the same scenario opened, its plans generated again (regenerating keeps every edit, `withAiPlan`), saved as "<name>, plans with seats". 16.5's check then prints, for each, the recommended leads: how many distinct and how many on the most named. WP8 does what it should when the most-named lead holds about as many of the new seats as Stage 1's proposals give one person, not most of them. A client edit between the runs changes the book a little; the comparison holds unless it touches one of the scenario's clients.

**Cost, estimated until the check prints the saved plans' own averages.** Each run is one plan per affected client: about 14 for a partner with an average book (84 clients over six partners). Per plan: the book at about 22,000 characters for 84 clients (17.4's 16 clients gave 13,477; about 120 characters a client row), so about 5,500 tokens, written at 1.25x ($2.50 a million) by the first two plans and read at 0.1x ($0.20) by the rest; the user turn about 1,000 to 1,300 tokens at $2; the output, adaptive thinking at `high` and a plan of about 600 words, 2,000 to 4,000 tokens at $10. About $0.03 to $0.06 a plan, $0.40 to $0.85 a run, under $2 for the two. The AI budget is 30 requests a partner an hour (D11, T16): two runs of 14 by one partner in one hour would use 28, so split them between two partners or two hours. The answers are the firm's saved answers and never enter the repository.

(b), `scripts/eval-ai.cjs` re-asking a saved scenario's plans with and without seats, side by side, under its estimate and `--confirm`: the same book and the same minute for both, at half a session more and a second home for the plan's prompt. (c), no evaluation: a prompt change nobody measured.

- `utils/transitionPlan.cjs`: `checkSeats` and the section, `createTransitionPlanPrompt` taking `{ seats }`; `routes/scenarios.cjs`: the check after the roster's and its 400.
- `server.cjs`: `transition-plan-seats` in `/api/health`'s features (thirteen).
- `src/utils/transitionPlans.js`: `planRequest(..., { seats })`; `ClientReviewInterface.jsx`: `seats` sent when `/api/health` lists the feature, and WP12's line reworded.
- `tests/transition-plan.test.mjs`, `tests/transition-plans.test.mjs` and `tests/routes.test.mjs` as 17.7; `tests/import-db.test.mjs`'s expected features.
- Docs: `CLAUDE.md` (the route's request, the feature name, Stage 2), runbook section 10's feature list, this file's section 2.
- No new route (the inventory stays at 29), no schema, no new log line; the response, the saved answer and the plan's state unchanged.

**Not in this package:** loads after the scenario's seats (U37 (b)); plans written one at a time (U11 (c)); structured output; anything about a hire scenario (it asks the AI for nothing, Tier 2 section 14).

### 17.7 Tests

Each new test is shown failing first against `origin/main`'s code, in a worktree with only the test files copied in (0.2 item 6): today's route ignores `seats`, so each refusal answers 200 there and the section is absent.

- `tests/transition-plan.test.mjs`: `checkSeats`, each refusal with its message, absent `seats` and `[]` accepted; only the seats that change hands described (a staying lead whose second-chair seat was vacated is not "would lead"; a filled second-chair seat is described; a client left without a lead); grouped by person in the roster's order; no figure in the section (no `$`, effort or count); the prompt without `seats` equal to today's, byte for byte, on the fixture book for every client and two departures (a frozen copy of today's output as a fixture); the system blocks equal with and without `seats`.
- `tests/transition-plans.test.mjs`: `planRequest` with `{ seats: true }`: one entry per other decision, the person ids as text, the partner's choices over the proposals, the plan's own client left out; without the option, the body as today.
- `tests/routes.test.mjs`, `POST /api/scenarios/transition-plan`'s block, both table shapes: the fake's recorded request with seats carrying the section and its system blocks byte for byte those of the same request without seats; each refusal 400, with nothing sent to the fake and nothing saved; `/api/health` listing `transition-plan-seats` (`FEATURES`, `:177`); the inventory at 29. `tests/import-db.test.mjs`: its expected features.
- `tests/ai-grep.test.mjs` unchanged: one call site, `cache_control` only where it is.
- End to end, scratch scripts as for every package: Stage 2 in headless Chromium against a local `server.cjs` and the fake: the page sending `seats` with the feature and none against `origin/main`'s server; "Use these picks" on one client and then plans regenerated for another, whose request carries the new seat; WP12's line in both forms.

### 17.8 Acceptance

The gates green: lint 0 errors and the 3 warnings; `npm test` 538 plus the new tests; `build:prod` with no CSS or config warning; `npm audit` 0 and 0. The database suites at the baselines but `routes` up by its new tests (67, 72, 362 plus them with 29 routes, 33). 17.7's failing-first run and end-to-end run; then, after the deploy, 17.5's evaluation.

### 17.9 Traps

1. **The seats are the scenario's at the moment of each request.** Two at a time (D9), every plan of a first run sees Stage 1's proposals for the others; a pick made after a plan was written ("Use these picks" on one client) is in no plan written before it. Regenerating a plan after picks sends the seats as they now are. WP12's line says so.
2. **Describe the changes only.** A client whose lead stays and whose second-chair seat was vacated must not read "would lead"; compare each seat with the book entry's (the prototype's rule).
3. **No figures in the section** (T14 as U11 (b) words it): loads after the seats are U37 (b), not a passing addition.
4. **Never in the system blocks** (T8): the section differs per request, and in the system blocks it would make every plan write its own cache entry and leave Ask's.
5. **Ids as text, never cast** (constraint 1): the person ids as the state holds them; the client id compared as text with the book's.
6. **The order of refusals**: the request's 400, the book's 404, the roster's 400, the seats' 400, then the model. A refused request counts once on the AI limiters, as any 400 after them does (they are mounted on the route, `routes/scenarios.cjs:75`).
7. **The plan's own client** is never in `seats` (U38 (a)); the server refuses it rather than quietly dropping it.
8. **The fake reads the roster by its lines' shape** (`- <name> (<Role>): leads `, `tests/helpers/fakeAnthropic.mjs:160`): the section's lines must not take that shape, or the fake would count them as roster entries; the prototype's read "would lead" and do not.
9. **The feature name in three places:** `server.cjs:153`, `tests/routes.test.mjs:177` and `tests/import-db.test.mjs`'s list.
10. **Thirty an hour.** A Stage 2 run spends one request a plan, as now; 17.5's two runs need splitting across partners or hours.

### 17.10 Mixed versions and rollback

The page sends `seats` only to an API that lists `transition-plan-seats` (constraint 5). The page published first, on an older API: no feature name, no seats sent, the plans as today, and WP12's line in its first form. An older page on the new API sends no `seats`, and the prompt is today's byte for byte (17.4). Render rolled back alone: the page asks `/api/health` before every run and stops sending; Netlify rolled back alone: the older page sends none. Nothing stored changes and there is no schema: a saved plan does not record whether it saw the seats (the prompt is not saved), and its date against the deploy's says which it was.

In Scenarios, someone who leads several clients leaving, Stage 2's plans generated: WP12's line in its second form; each plan's `ai_call` line with `inputTokens` a few hundred above a plan's before the deploy and cache reads from the third plan on. Then 17.5's evaluation (U39 (a)) and 16.5's check, its "most on the most named" for the two saved scenarios; the next session records both in section 2.

Stage 1's proposals spread the load."
